> AGENT

Here's the someguy sidecar status on staging:

**Container:** `cipherbox-staging-someguy-1`
- **Status:** Running, healthy (up 31 hours)
- **Image:** `ghcr.io/ipfs/someguy:v0.11.1` (built 2026-02-16)
- **Created:** 2026-03-08 (2 weeks ago)

**Resource Usage:**
- CPU: 7.16% (capped at 0.5 cores)
- Memory: 288.9 MiB / 768 MiB (37.6%)
- Network I/O: 7.43 GB in / 13.8 GB out
- PIDs: 12

**Configuration:**
- DHT mode: `standard`
- Listen: `0.0.0.0:8190` (not published to host, only accessible within Docker network at `172.18.0.7`)
- Connection manager: low=50, high=300
- Max memory: 512 MiB
- Restart policy: `unless-stopped`
- Health check: `curl -sf http://localhost:8190/version` every 10s

**DHT Routing Table:** ~169 peers, actively refreshing CPLs

**API Status:** Working — provider lookups via `/routing/v1/providers/` return results with multiple peers (Pinata, Filebase, dag.house, etc.)

**Logs:** Normal DHT maintenance. Some `failed negotiate identify protocol` warnings (typical for DHT — remote peers at resource limits). No errors or restarts.

Overall it looks healthy and functioning well.

```
name: cipherbox-staging

x-logging: &default-logging
  driver: json-file
  options:
    max-size: '10m'
    max-file: '3'

services:
  api:
    image: ghcr.io/${GITHUB_REPOSITORY_OWNER:-OWNER}/cipherbox-api:${TAG:-latest}
    restart: unless-stopped
    env_file: .env.staging
    ports:
      - '127.0.0.1:3000:3000'
    logging: *default-logging
    depends_on:
      postgres:
        condition: service_healthy
      redis:
        condition: service_healthy

  postgres:
    image: postgres:16-alpine
    restart: unless-stopped
    environment:
      POSTGRES_USER: ${DB_USERNAME:-cipherbox}
      POSTGRES_PASSWORD: ${DB_PASSWORD}
      POSTGRES_DB: ${DB_DATABASE:-cipherbox_staging}
    ports:
      - '127.0.0.1:5432:5432'
    volumes:
      - postgres_staging:/var/lib/postgresql/data
    logging: *default-logging
    healthcheck:
      test:
        [
          'CMD-SHELL',
          'pg_isready -U ${DB_USERNAME:-cipherbox} -d ${DB_DATABASE:-cipherbox_staging}',
        ]
      interval: 5s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 5

  redis:
    image: redis:7-alpine
    restart: unless-stopped
    command: ['redis-server', '--requirepass', '${REDIS_PASSWORD}']
    environment:
      REDISCLI_AUTH: ${REDIS_PASSWORD}
    ports:
      - '127.0.0.1:6379:6379'
    volumes:
      - redis_staging:/data
    logging: *default-logging
    healthcheck:
      test: ['CMD', 'redis-cli', 'ping']
      interval: 5s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 5

  ipfs:
    image: ipfs/kubo:v0.34.0
    restart: unless-stopped
    environment:
      IPFS_PROFILE: server
    ports:
      - '4001:4001/tcp'
      - '4001:4001/udp'
      - '127.0.0.1:5001:5001'
      - '127.0.0.1:8080:8080'
    volumes:
      - ipfs_staging:/data/ipfs
    logging: *default-logging
    healthcheck:
      test: ['CMD-SHELL', 'ipfs id || exit 1']
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 10
      start_period: 30s
    deploy:
      resources:
        limits:
          memory: 2G
          cpus: '1.0'

  someguy:
    image: ghcr.io/ipfs/someguy:v0.11.1
    restart: unless-stopped
    environment:
      SOMEGUY_LISTEN_ADDRESS: 0.0.0.0:8190
      SOMEGUY_DHT: standard
      SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_CONNMGR_LOW: 50
      SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_CONNMGR_HIGH: 300
      SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_MAX_MEMORY: 536870912 # 512MB in bytes
      GOLOG_LOG_LEVEL: info
      GOLOG_LOG_FMT: json
    logging: *default-logging
    healthcheck:
      test: ['CMD-SHELL', 'curl -sf http://localhost:8190/version || exit 1']
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 10
      start_period: 30s
    deploy:
      resources:
        limits:
          memory: 768M
          cpus: '0.5'

  tee-worker:
    image: ghcr.io/${GITHUB_REPOSITORY_OWNER:-OWNER}/cipherbox-tee-worker:${TAG:-latest}
    restart: unless-stopped
    environment:
      NODE_ENV: production
      PORT: 3001
      TEE_MODE: simulator
      CIPHERBOX_ENVIRONMENT: staging
      TEE_WORKER_SECRET: ${TEE_WORKER_SECRET}
    ports:
      - '127.0.0.1:3001:3001'
    logging: *default-logging
    healthcheck:
      test: ['CMD-SHELL', 'wget -qO- http://localhost:3001/health || exit 1']
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 5

  caddy:
    image: caddy:2-alpine
    restart: unless-stopped
    ports:
      - '80:80'
      - '443:443'
    volumes:
      - ./Caddyfile:/etc/caddy/Caddyfile
      - ./certs:/etc/caddy/certs
      - /opt/cipherbox/web:/srv/web:ro
      - caddy_data:/data
      - caddy_config:/config
    logging: *default-logging

  alloy:
    image: grafana/alloy:v1.6.1
    restart: unless-stopped
    volumes:
      - ./alloy-config.river:/etc/alloy/config.river
      - /var/run/docker.sock:/var/run/docker.sock:ro
    environment:
      GRAFANA_LOKI_URL: ${GRAFANA_LOKI_URL}
      GRAFANA_LOKI_USERNAME: ${GRAFANA_LOKI_USERNAME}
      GRAFANA_LOKI_API_KEY: ${GRAFANA_LOKI_API_KEY}
      GRAFANA_PROMETHEUS_URL: ${GRAFANA_PROMETHEUS_URL}
      GRAFANA_PROMETHEUS_USERNAME: ${GRAFANA_PROMETHEUS_USERNAME}
      GRAFANA_PROMETHEUS_API_KEY: ${GRAFANA_PROMETHEUS_API_KEY}
    command:
      - run
      - /etc/alloy/config.river
    logging: *default-logging
    depends_on:
      - api

volumes:
  postgres_staging:
  redis_staging:
  ipfs_staging:
  caddy_data:
  caddy_config:
```

     4→/** Typed error for non-retryable HTTP responses from the delegated routing API. */
     5→class DelegatedRoutingHttpError extends Error {

     7→    super(`Delegated routing returned ${status}`);
     8→    this.name = 'DelegatedRoutingHttpError';

    11→
    12→@Injectable()
    13→export class DelegatedRoutingClient {
    14→  private readonly logger = new Logger(DelegatedRoutingClient.name);
    15→  private readonly delegatedRoutingUrl: string;
    16→  private readonly maxRetries = 3;
    17→  private readonly baseDelayMs = 1000;
    18→  private readonly maxRetryDelayMs = 30_000;
    19→  private readonly requestTimeoutMs = 10_000;
    20→
    21→  constructor(private readonly configService: ConfigService) {

    23→      'DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL',
    24→      'https://delegated-ipfs.dev'
    25→    );
    26→  }
    27→
    28→  /**
    29→   * Publish an IPNS record via the delegated routing PUT API with exponential backoff retry.
    30→   * Throws HttpException (BAD_GATEWAY) on persistent failures.
    31→   */
    32→  async publish(ipnsName: string, recordBytes: Uint8Array): Promise<void> {
    33→    const url = `${this.delegatedRoutingUrl}/routing/v1/ipns/${encodeURIComponent(ipnsName)}`;
    34→
    35→    let lastError: Error | null = null;
    36→
    37→    for (let attempt = 0; attempt < this.maxRetries; attempt++) {
    38→      try {
    39→        const { ok, status, retryAfter, errorText } = await this.fetchWithTimeout(
    40→          url,

    42→            method: 'PUT',
    43→            headers: {
    44→              'Content-Type': 'application/vnd.ipfs.ipns-record',
    45→            },
    46→            body: recordBytes as unknown as BodyInit,
    47→          },
    48→          async (response) => ({
    49→            ok: response.ok,
    50→            status: response.status,
    51→            retryAfter: response.headers.get('Retry-After'),
    52→            errorText: !response.ok && response.status !== 429 ? await response.text() : '',
    53→          })
    54→        );
    55→
    56→        if (ok) {
    57→          this.logger.log(`IPNS record published successfully for ${ipnsName}`);
    58→          return;
    59→        }
    60→
    61→        // Handle rate limiting
    62→        if (status === 429) {
    63→          if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {
    64→            const delayMs = this.getRetryDelayMs(retryAfter, attempt);
    65→
    66→            this.logger.warn(`Rate limited on IPNS publish, retrying in ${delayMs}ms`);
    67→            await this.delay(delayMs);
    68→          }
    69→          continue;
    70→        }
    71→
    72→        // Non-retryable HTTP error

    74→        this.logger.error(`Delegated routing returned ${status} for ${ipnsName}: ${errorText}`);
    75→        throw new DelegatedRoutingHttpError(status);
    76→      } catch (error) {

    80→          throw new HttpException(

    95→        }
    96→      }
    97→    }
    98→

   114→  async resolve(ipnsName: string): Promise<Uint8Array | null> {

   116→

   133→            data: response.ok ? new Uint8Array(await response.arrayBuffer()) : null,
   134→            errorText:
   135→              !response.ok && response.status !== 404 && response.status !== 429
   136→                ? await response.text()
   137→                : '',
   138→          })
   139→        );
   140→
   141→        // 404 means IPNS name not found - not an error
   142→        if (status === 404) {
   143→          this.logger.debug(`IPNS name not found: ${ipnsName}`);
   144→          return null;
   145→        }
   146→
   147→        if (ok) {
   148→          return data;
   149→        }
   150→
   151→        // Handle rate limiting
   152→        if (status === 429) {
   153→          if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {
   154→            const delayMs = this.getRetryDelayMs(retryAfter, attempt);
   155→
   156→            this.logger.warn(`Rate limited on IPNS resolve, retrying in ${delayMs}ms`);
   157→            await this.delay(delayMs);
   158→          }

   160→        }
   161→
   162→        // Non-retryable error
   163→        // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error details but don't expose to client
   164→        this.logger.error(
   165→          `Delegated routing resolution returned ${status} for ${ipnsName}: ${errorText}`
   166→        );
   167→        throw new DelegatedRoutingHttpError(status);
   168→      } catch (error) {
   169→        // Re-throw HttpException immediately (e.g., parsing errors) - don't retry
   170→        if (error instanceof HttpException) {
   171→          throw error;
   172→        }
   173→
   174→        // Non-retryable HTTP error — surface immediately
   175→        if (error instanceof DelegatedRoutingHttpError) {
   176→          // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Generic error message to avoid leaking internal details
   177→          throw new HttpException(
   178→            'Failed to resolve IPNS name from routing network',

   180→          );
   181→        }
   182→
   183→        lastError = error instanceof Error ? error : new Error(String(error));
   184→
   185→        // Exponential backoff for network errors
   186→        if (attempt < this.maxRetries - 1) {
   187→          const delayMs = this.baseDelayMs * Math.pow(2, attempt);
   188→          this.logger.warn(

   190→          );
   191→          await this.delay(delayMs);
   192→        }
   193→      }
   194→    }

   196→    // [SECURITY: MEDIUM-11] Log full error, return generic message
   197→    this.logger.error(

   199→    );
   200→    throw new HttpException(
   201→      'Failed to resolve IPNS name from routing network after multiple attempts',
   202→      HttpStatus.BAD_GATEWAY
   203→    );
   204→  }
   205→
   206→  private async fetchWithTimeout<T>(
   207→    url: string,
   208→    init: RequestInit,
   209→    consume: (response: Response) => Promise<T>
   210→  ): Promise<T> {
   211→    const controller = new AbortController();
   212→    const timeout = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), this.requestTimeoutMs);
   213→    try {
   214→      const response = await fetch(url, { ...init, signal: controller.signal });
   215→      return await consume(response);
   216→    } finally {
   217→      clearTimeout(timeout);
   218→    }
   219→  }
   220→
   221→  private getRetryDelayMs(retryAfter: string | null, attempt: number): number {
   222→    const fallback = this.baseDelayMs * Math.pow(2, attempt);
   223→    if (!retryAfter) return Math.min(fallback, this.maxRetryDelayMs);
   224→
   225→    const seconds = Number(retryAfter);
   226→    if (Number.isFinite(seconds) && seconds >= 0) {
   227→      return Math.min(seconds * 1000, this.maxRetryDelayMs);
   228→    }
   229→
   230→    const retryAt = Date.parse(retryAfter);
   231→    if (!Number.isNaN(retryAt)) {
   232→      return Math.min(Math.max(0, retryAt - Date.now()), this.maxRetryDelayMs);
   233→    }
   234→
   235→    return Math.min(fallback, this.maxRetryDelayMs);
   236→  }
   237→
   238→  private delay(ms: number): Promise<void> {
   239→    return new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));
   240→  }
   241→}
   242→

> AGENT

I don't see any evidence in git history of someguy being disabled. The code has a fallback default of `https://delegated-ipfs.dev` (line 24), but on staging the env var is set to `http://someguy:8190`.

So the current state is:

- **Someguy is running AND actively being used** by the API for both IPNS publish and resolve operations
- `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL=http://someguy:8190` is set in `.env.staging`
- There's no commit or config change that ever switched it back to `delegated-ipfs.dev`

It's possible the "disable" happened outside of git — e.g., you may have asked me to change the `.env.staging` file on the server directly, but it was later overwritten by a deployment that reset it to the compose/env defaults. Or it may have been a conversation that didn't result in a persistent change.

Would you like me to:
1. **Check the actual performance now** — run some timed IPNS resolve/publish calls through the API to compare against the phase 18 metrics?
2. **Switch back to delegated-ipfs.dev** — update `.env.staging` on the server to bypass someguy?
3. **Stop the someguy container** to save resources if you don't want it running?

> AGENT

Good to know. So it sounds like the disable never actually stuck or was reverted by a subsequent deploy. Given that it's actively handling routing for the API right now, would you like me to do a quick performance comparison — time some IPNS resolves through someguy vs delegated-ipfs.dev to see if the performance gap you noticed before is still there? That would help decide whether to keep it or switch back.

     1→name: Deploy Staging
     2→
     3→on:
     4→  push:
     5→    tags:
     6→      - 'v*-staging*'
     7→  workflow_call:
     8→    inputs:
     9→      staging_tag:
    10→        required: true
    11→        type: string
    12→
    13→env:
    14→  REGISTRY: ghcr.io
    15→  # Resolve tag: workflow_call passes it as input, push trigger uses ref_name
    16→  DEPLOY_TAG: ${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}
    17→
    18→jobs:
    19→  build-api:
    20→    name: Build & Push API Image
    21→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    22→    permissions:
    23→      contents: read
    24→      packages: write
    25→    steps:
    26→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
    27→        with:
    28→          ref: ${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}
    29→
    30→      - name: Set lowercase image name
    31→        run: echo "API_IMAGE=ghcr.io/${GITHUB_REPOSITORY_OWNER,,}/cipherbox-api" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"
    32→
    33→      - uses: docker/login-action@v3
    34→        with:
    35→          registry: ghcr.io
    36→          username: ${{ github.actor }}
    37→          password: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
    38→
    39→      - uses: docker/build-push-action@v5
    40→        with:
    41→          context: .
    42→          file: apps/api/Dockerfile
    43→          push: true
    44→          tags: |
    45→            ${{ env.API_IMAGE }}:${{ env.DEPLOY_TAG }}
    46→            ${{ env.API_IMAGE }}:latest
    47→
    48→  build-tee:
    49→    name: Build & Push TEE Worker Image
    50→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    51→    permissions:
    52→      contents: read
    53→      packages: write
    54→    steps:
    55→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
    56→        with:
    57→          ref: ${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}
    58→
    59→      - name: Set lowercase image name
    60→        run: echo "TEE_IMAGE=ghcr.io/${GITHUB_REPOSITORY_OWNER,,}/cipherbox-tee-worker" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"
    61→
    62→      - uses: docker/login-action@v3
    63→        with:
    64→          registry: ghcr.io
    65→          username: ${{ github.actor }}
    66→          password: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
    67→
    68→      - uses: docker/build-push-action@v5
    69→        with:
    70→          context: ./tee-worker
    71→          file: tee-worker/Dockerfile
    72→          push: true
    73→          tags: |
    74→            ${{ env.TEE_IMAGE }}:${{ env.DEPLOY_TAG }}
    75→            ${{ env.TEE_IMAGE }}:latest
    76→
    77→  build-web:
    78→    name: Build Web App
    79→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    80→    environment: staging
    81→    steps:
    82→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
    83→        with:
    84→          ref: ${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}
    85→
    86→      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4
    87→        with:
    88→          version: 10
    89→
    90→      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
    91→        with:
    92→          node-version: '22'
    93→          cache: 'pnpm'
    94→
    95→      - name: Install dependencies
    96→        run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
    97→
    98→      - name: Build shared packages
    99→        run: |
   100→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/crypto build
   101→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/core build
   102→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api-client build
   103→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk-core build
   104→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk build
   105→
   106→      - name: Build web app with staging env vars
   107→        run: pnpm --filter @cipherbox/web build
   108→        env:
   109→          VITE_WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID: ${{ vars.VITE_WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID }}
   110→          VITE_API_URL: ${{ vars.STAGING_API_URL }}
   111→          VITE_ENVIRONMENT: staging
   112→          VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID: ${{ vars.GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID }}
   113→
   114→      - name: Upload web dist as artifact
   115→        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
   116→        with:
   117→          name: web-dist
   118→          path: apps/web/dist/
   119→          retention-days: 1
   120→
   121→  create-desktop-release:
   122→    name: Create Desktop Release
   123→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
   124→    permissions:
   125→      contents: write
   126→    steps:
   127→      - name: Create or update GitHub Release
   128→        env:
   129→          GH_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
   130→        run: |
   131→          TAG="${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}"
   132→          BODY="Staging build for ${TAG}. macOS (unsigned - right-click > Open to launch), Windows (unsigned - click \"More info\" > \"Run anyway\"), and Linux (AppImage/deb)."
   133→          # Create the release if it doesn't exist yet; ignore error if it does
   134→          gh release create "$TAG" \
   135→            --repo "${{ github.repository }}" \
   136→            --title "CipherBox Desktop ${TAG}" \
   137→            --notes "$BODY" \
   138→            --prerelease 2>/dev/null || true
   139→
   140→  build-desktop:
   141→    name: Build Desktop App (macOS)
   142→    needs: [create-desktop-release]
   143→    runs-on: macos-latest
   144→    environment: staging
   145→    permissions:
   146→      contents: write
   147→    steps:
   148→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
   149→        with:
   150→          ref: ${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}
   151→
   152→      - name: Install FUSE-T
   153→        run: |
   154→          brew install --cask fuse-t
   155→          sudo mkdir -p /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig
   156→          sudo ln -sf "/Library/Application Support/fuse-t/pkgconfig/fuse-t.pc" /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig/fuse.pc
   157→
   158→      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4
   159→        with:
   160→          version: 10
   161→
   162→      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
   163→        with:
   164→          node-version: '22'
   165→          cache: 'pnpm'
   166→
   167→      - name: Install dependencies
   168→        run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
   169→
   170→      - name: Build shared packages
   171→        run: |
   172→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/crypto build
   173→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/core build
   174→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api-client build
   175→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk-core build
   176→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk build
   177→
   178→      - uses: tauri-apps/tauri-action@v0
   179→        env:
   180→          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
   181→          VITE_WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID: ${{ vars.VITE_WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID }}
   182→          VITE_API_URL: ${{ vars.STAGING_API_URL }}
   183→          VITE_ENVIRONMENT: staging
   184→          VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID: ${{ vars.GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID }}
   185→        with:
   186→          projectPath: apps/desktop
   187→          tauriScript: pnpm tauri
   188→          tagName: ${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}
   189→          releaseName: 'CipherBox Desktop ${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}'
   190→          releaseBody: 'Staging build for ${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}. macOS (unsigned - right-click > Open to launch) and Windows (unsigned - click "More info" > "Run anyway").'
   191→          releaseDraft: false
   192→          prerelease: true
   193→
   194→  build-desktop-windows:
   195→    name: Build Desktop App (Windows)
   196→    needs: [create-desktop-release]
   197→    runs-on: windows-latest
   198→    environment: staging
   199→    permissions:
   200→      contents: write
   201→    steps:
   202→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
   203→        with:
   204→          ref: ${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}
   205→
   206→      - name: Install WinFsp
   207→        shell: powershell
   208→        run: |
   209→          $url = "https://github.com/winfsp/winfsp/releases/download/v2.1/winfsp-2.1.25156.msi"
   210→          # Use Join-Path to produce proper Windows backslash paths —
   211→          # msiexec.exe treats '/' as a flag prefix, so forward slashes in
   212→          # the file path cause "package could not be opened" errors.
   213→          $resourceDir = Join-Path "${{ github.workspace }}" "apps\desktop\src-tauri\resources"
   214→          $out = Join-Path $resourceDir "winfsp-2.1.25156.msi"
   215→          $expectedHash = "073A70E00F77423E34BED98B86E600DEF93393BA5822204FAC57A29324DB9F7A"
   216→          New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $resourceDir
   217→          Invoke-WebRequest -Uri $url -OutFile $out
   218→          $actualHash = (Get-FileHash $out -Algorithm SHA256).Hash
   219→          if ($actualHash -ne $expectedHash) { throw "WinFsp MSI hash mismatch: expected $expectedHash, got $actualHash" }
   220→          $proc = Start-Process msiexec.exe -ArgumentList "/i","`"$out`"","/qn","INSTALLLEVEL=1000" -Wait -NoNewWindow -PassThru
   221→          if ($proc.ExitCode -ne 0) { throw "msiexec failed with exit code $($proc.ExitCode)" }
   222→          # winfsp-sys build.rs reads HKLM\SOFTWARE\WOW6432Node\WinFsp\InstallDir
   223→          # to find headers/libs. The MSI doesn't reliably write this key on CI runners.
   224→          $installDir = "C:\Program Files (x86)\WinFsp"
   225→          if (!(Test-Path "HKLM:\SOFTWARE\WOW6432Node\WinFsp")) {
   226→            New-Item -Path "HKLM:\SOFTWARE\WOW6432Node\WinFsp" -Force | Out-Null
   227→          }
   228→          New-ItemProperty -Path "HKLM:\SOFTWARE\WOW6432Node\WinFsp" -Name "InstallDir" -Value $installDir -PropertyType String -Force | Out-Null
   229→          # Verify registry key was written correctly
   230→          $regVal = Get-ItemProperty -Path "HKLM:\SOFTWARE\WOW6432Node\WinFsp" -Name "InstallDir" -ErrorAction Stop
   231→          if (-not $regVal.InstallDir -or $regVal.InstallDir -ne $installDir) {
   232→            throw "WinFsp InstallDir registry mismatch: expected '$installDir', got '$($regVal.InstallDir)'"
   233→          }
   234→          Write-Host "WinFsp InstallDir registry: $($regVal.InstallDir)"
   235→          if (!(Test-Path "$installDir\lib")) { throw "WinFsp lib directory not found at $installDir\lib" }
   236→          if (!(Test-Path "$installDir\inc")) { throw "WinFsp inc directory not found at $installDir\inc" }
   237→
   238→      - name: Install Rust toolchain
   239→        run: rustup default stable
   240→
   241→      - uses: actions/cache@v4
   242→        with:
   243→          path: |
   244→            ~/.cargo/registry
   245→            ~/.cargo/git
   246→            apps/desktop/src-tauri/target
   247→          key: windows-cargo-${{ hashFiles('apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.lock') }}
   248→          restore-keys: windows-cargo-
   249→
   250→      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4
   251→        with:
   252→          version: 10
   253→
   254→      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
   255→        with:
   256→          node-version: '22'
   257→          cache: 'pnpm'
   258→
   259→      - name: Install dependencies
   260→        run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
   261→
   262→      - name: Build shared packages
   263→        run: |
   264→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/crypto build
   265→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/core build
   266→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api-client build
   267→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk-core build
   268→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk build
   269→
   270→      - uses: tauri-apps/tauri-action@v0
   271→        env:
   272→          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
   273→          VITE_WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID: ${{ vars.VITE_WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID }}
   274→          VITE_API_URL: ${{ vars.STAGING_API_URL }}
   275→          VITE_ENVIRONMENT: staging
   276→          VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID: ${{ vars.GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID }}
   277→        with:
   278→          projectPath: apps/desktop
   279→          tauriScript: pnpm tauri
   280→          tagName: ${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}
   281→          releaseName: 'CipherBox Desktop ${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}'
   282→          releaseBody: 'Staging build for ${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}. macOS (unsigned - right-click > Open to launch), Windows (unsigned - click "More info" > "Run anyway"), and Linux (AppImage/deb).'
   283→          releaseDraft: false
   284→          prerelease: true
   285→          args: -- --no-default-features --features winfsp
   286→
   287→  build-desktop-linux:
   288→    name: Build Desktop App (Linux)
   289→    needs: [create-desktop-release]
   290→    runs-on: ubuntu-22.04
   291→    environment: staging
   292→    permissions:
   293→      contents: write
   294→    steps:
   295→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
   296→        with:
   297→          ref: ${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}
   298→
   299→      - name: Install system dependencies
   300→        run: |
   301→          sudo apt-get update
   302→          sudo apt-get install -y \
   303→            libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev \
   304→            libayatana-appindicator3-dev \
   305→            librsvg2-dev \
   306→            libssl-dev \
   307→            libxdo-dev \
   308→            libfuse3-dev \
   309→            pkg-config \
   310→            build-essential
   311→
   312→      - name: Install Rust toolchain
   313→        run: rustup default stable
   314→
   315→      - uses: actions/cache@v4
   316→        with:
   317→          path: |
   318→            ~/.cargo/registry
   319→            ~/.cargo/git
   320→            apps/desktop/src-tauri/target
   321→          key: linux-cargo-${{ hashFiles('apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.lock') }}
   322→          restore-keys: linux-cargo-
   323→
   324→      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4
   325→        with:
   326→          version: 10
   327→
   328→      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
   329→        with:
   330→          node-version: '22'
   331→          cache: 'pnpm'
   332→
   333→      - name: Install dependencies
   334→        run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
   335→
   336→      - name: Build shared packages
   337→        run: |
   338→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/crypto build
   339→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/core build
   340→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api-client build
   341→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk-core build
   342→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk build
   343→
   344→      - uses: tauri-apps/tauri-action@v0
   345→        env:
   346→          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
   347→          VITE_WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID: ${{ vars.VITE_WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID }}
   348→          VITE_API_URL: ${{ vars.STAGING_API_URL }}
   349→          VITE_ENVIRONMENT: staging
   350→          VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID: ${{ vars.GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID }}
   351→        with:
   352→          projectPath: apps/desktop
   353→          tauriScript: pnpm tauri
   354→          tagName: ${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}
   355→          releaseName: 'CipherBox Desktop ${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}'
   356→          releaseBody: 'Staging build for ${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}. macOS (unsigned - right-click > Open to launch), Windows (unsigned - click "More info" > "Run anyway"), and Linux (AppImage/deb).'
   357→          releaseDraft: false
   358→          prerelease: true
   359→          args: -- --no-default-features --features fuse
   360→
   361→  deploy-vps:
   362→    name: Deploy to Staging VPS
   363→    needs: [build-api, build-tee, build-web]
   364→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
   365→    environment: staging
   366→    permissions:
   367→      contents: read
   368→      packages: read
   369→    steps:
   370→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
   371→        with:
   372→          ref: ${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}
   373→
   374→      - name: Download web dist artifact
   375→        uses: actions/download-artifact@v4
   376→        with:
   377→          name: web-dist
   378→          path: web-dist/
   379→
   380→      - name: Generate .env.staging
   381→        run: |
   382→          cat > .env.staging << 'ENVEOF'
   383→          NODE_ENV=staging
   384→          CIPHERBOX_ENVIRONMENT=staging
   385→          TEST_LOGIN_SECRET=${{ secrets.STAGING_TEST_LOGIN_SECRET }}
   386→          PORT=3000
   387→          DB_HOST=postgres
   388→          DB_PORT=5432
   389→          DB_USERNAME=${{ vars.STAGING_DB_USERNAME }}
   390→          DB_PASSWORD=${{ secrets.STAGING_DB_PASSWORD }}
   391→          DB_DATABASE=cipherbox_staging
   392→          JWT_SECRET=${{ secrets.STAGING_JWT_SECRET }}
   393→          CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS=${{ vars.CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS }}
   394→          IPFS_PROVIDER=local
   395→          IPFS_LOCAL_API_URL=http://ipfs:5001
   396→          IPFS_LOCAL_GATEWAY_URL=http://ipfs:8080
   397→          DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL=http://someguy:8190
   398→          REDIS_HOST=redis
   399→          REDIS_PORT=6379
   400→          TEE_WORKER_URL=http://tee-worker:3001
   401→          TEE_WORKER_SECRET=${{ secrets.STAGING_TEE_WORKER_SECRET }}
   402→          REDIS_PASSWORD=${{ secrets.STAGING_REDIS_PASSWORD }}
   403→          GRAFANA_LOKI_URL=${{ vars.GRAFANA_LOKI_URL }}
   404→          GRAFANA_LOKI_USERNAME=${{ vars.GRAFANA_LOKI_USERNAME }}
   405→          GRAFANA_LOKI_API_KEY=${{ secrets.GRAFANA_LOKI_API_KEY }}
   406→          GRAFANA_PROMETHEUS_URL=${{ vars.GRAFANA_PROMETHEUS_URL }}
   407→          GRAFANA_PROMETHEUS_USERNAME=${{ vars.GRAFANA_PROMETHEUS_USERNAME }}
   408→          GRAFANA_PROMETHEUS_API_KEY=${{ secrets.GRAFANA_PROMETHEUS_API_KEY }}
   409→          SENDGRID_API_KEY=${{ secrets.SENDGRID_API_KEY }}
   410→          SENDGRID_FROM_EMAIL=${{ vars.SENDGRID_FROM_EMAIL }}
   411→          GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID=${{ vars.GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID }}
   412→          IDENTITY_JWT_PRIVATE_KEY=${{ secrets.IDENTITY_JWT_PRIVATE_KEY }}
   413→          ENVEOF
   414→          # Append image tag and lowercase owner (can't use shell vars in quoted heredoc)
   415→          OWNER="${{ github.repository_owner }}"
   416→          echo "GITHUB_REPOSITORY_OWNER=${OWNER,,}" >> .env.staging
   417→          echo "TAG=${{ env.DEPLOY_TAG }}" >> .env.staging
   418→
   419→      - name: Copy files to VPS
   420→        uses: appleboy/scp-action@v0.1.7
   421→        with:
   422→          host: ${{ vars.STAGING_HOST }}
   423→          username: ${{ vars.STAGING_USER }}
   424→          key: ${{ secrets.STAGING_SSH_KEY }}
   425→          source: '.env.staging,docker/docker-compose.staging.yml,docker/Caddyfile,docker/alloy-config.river'
   426→          target: /opt/cipherbox
   427→          strip_components: 0
   428→
   429→      - name: Arrange files on VPS
   430→        uses: appleboy/ssh-action@v1.2.0
   431→        with:
   432→          host: ${{ vars.STAGING_HOST }}
   433→          username: ${{ vars.STAGING_USER }}
   434→          key: ${{ secrets.STAGING_SSH_KEY }}
   435→          script: |
   436→            # Move files to correct locations
   437→            mv /opt/cipherbox/.env.staging /opt/cipherbox/docker/.env.staging 2>/dev/null || true
   438→            cd /opt/cipherbox/docker
   439→
   440→            # Docker Compose reads .env automatically for variable substitution
   441→            cp .env.staging .env
   442→
   443→            # Restrict env file permissions (contains secrets)
   444→            chmod 600 .env .env.staging
   445→
   446→            # Ensure certs symlink exists for Caddy
   447→            rm -rf /opt/cipherbox/docker/certs
   448→            ln -sf /opt/cipherbox/certs /opt/cipherbox/docker/certs
   449→
   450→      - name: Deploy web app files to VPS
   451→        uses: appleboy/scp-action@v0.1.7
   452→        with:
   453→          host: ${{ vars.STAGING_HOST }}
   454→          username: ${{ vars.STAGING_USER }}
   455→          key: ${{ secrets.STAGING_SSH_KEY }}
   456→          source: 'web-dist/*'
   457→          target: /opt/cipherbox/web
   458→          strip_components: 1
   459→          rm: true
   460→
   461→      - name: Deploy services
   462→        uses: appleboy/ssh-action@v1.2.0
   463→        with:
   464→          host: ${{ vars.STAGING_HOST }}
   465→          username: ${{ vars.STAGING_USER }}
   466→          key: ${{ secrets.STAGING_SSH_KEY }}
   467→          script: |
   468→            set -euo pipefail
   469→            cd /opt/cipherbox/docker
   470→
   471→            # Login to GHCR
   472→            echo "${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}" | docker login ghcr.io -u ${{ github.actor }} --password-stdin
   473→
   474→            # Set image tag from git tag (GHCR requires lowercase owner)
   475→            export TAG="${{ env.DEPLOY_TAG }}"
   476→            OWNER="${{ github.repository_owner }}"
   477→            export GITHUB_REPOSITORY_OWNER="${OWNER,,}"
   478→
   479→            # Pull new images
   480→            docker compose -f docker-compose.staging.yml pull
   481→
   482→            # Run database migrations before starting services
   483→            # (api service inherits env vars from env_file in compose)
   484→            docker compose -f docker-compose.staging.yml run --rm \
   485→              api node dist/run-migrations.js
   486→
   487→            # Start/restart services
   488→            docker compose -f docker-compose.staging.yml up -d
   489→
   490→            # Restart Caddy to pick up refreshed web files bind mount
   491→            # (scp rm:true recreates the host dir, breaking the old mount inode)
   492→            docker compose -f docker-compose.staging.yml restart caddy
   493→
   494→            # Cleanup old images
   495→            docker image prune -f
   496→
   497→  provision-dashboard:
   498→    name: Provision Grafana Dashboard
   499→    needs: [deploy-vps]
   500→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
   501→    environment: staging
   502→    permissions:
   503→      contents: read
   504→    steps:
   505→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
   506→        with:
   507→          ref: ${{ inputs.staging_tag || github.ref_name }}
   508→
   509→      - name: Push dashboard to Grafana Cloud
   510→        env:
   511→          GRAFANA_URL: ${{ vars.GRAFANA_URL }}
   512→          GRAFANA_API_KEY: ${{ secrets.GRAFANA_API_KEY }}
   513→          DS_METRICS_UID: ${{ vars.GRAFANA_DS_METRICS_UID }}
   514→          DS_LOGS_UID: ${{ vars.GRAFANA_DS_LOGS_UID }}
   515→        run: |
   516→          set -euo pipefail
   517→          : "${GRAFANA_URL:?GRAFANA_URL is required}"
   518→          : "${GRAFANA_API_KEY:?GRAFANA_API_KEY is required}"
   519→          : "${DS_METRICS_UID:?GRAFANA_DS_METRICS_UID is required}"
   520→          : "${DS_LOGS_UID:?GRAFANA_DS_LOGS_UID is required}"
   521→          GRAFANA_URL="${GRAFANA_URL%/}"
   522→
   523→          # The dashboard JSON uses __inputs for datasource templating.
   524→          # Replace variable references with actual datasource UIDs.
   525→          DASHBOARD=$(cat docker/grafana/dashboards/cipherbox-staging.json \
   526→            | jq --arg metrics "$DS_METRICS_UID" --arg logs "$DS_LOGS_UID" \
   527→                 'del(.__inputs)
   528→                  | .id = null
   529→                  | walk(if type == "object" and .uid? and (.uid | test("\\$\\{DS_")) then
   530→                      .uid = (if .uid == "${DS_GRAFANA_CLOUD_METRICS}" then $metrics
   531→                              elif .uid == "${DS_GRAFANA_CLOUD_LOGS}" then $logs
   532→                              else .uid end)
   533→                    else . end)')
   534→

> AGENT

Three unresolved threads. Let me triage them:

1. **Copilot #1** (line 514): Add env var validation — valid, easy fix
2. **Copilot #2** (line 525): Fail fast on 401/403/404 — valid, good suggestion
3. **CodeRabbit** (line 531): Deadline-based timeout — over-engineered for this use case. 30 attempts x (15s max curl + 10s sleep) = 12.5 min theoretical max, but in practice each curl returns in <1s. The attempt-based approach is clear enough and 5 min is a soft target, not a hard SLA. I'll skip the deadline rewrite but the other two fixes are worth making.

  "new_string": "        run: |\n          set -euo pipefail\n          : \"${GRAFANA_URL:?GRAFANA_URL is required for Grafana readiness check}\"\n          : \"${GRAFANA_API_KEY:?GRAFANA_API_KEY is required for Grafana readiness check}\"\n          GRAFANA_URL=\"${GRAFANA_URL%/}\"\n          MAX_ATTEMPTS=30\n          INTERVAL=10\n          for ((i=1; i<=MAX_ATTEMPTS; i++)); do\n            HTTP_CODE=$(curl -sS --connect-timeout 10 --max-time 15 \\\n              -o /dev/null -w \"%{http_code}\" \\\n              -H \"Authorization: Bearer ${GRAFANA_API_KEY}\" \\\n              \"${GRAFANA_URL}/api/health\") || HTTP_CODE=000\n            if [[ \"$HTTP_CODE\" == \"200\" ]]; then\n              echo \"Grafana Cloud is ready (attempt $i/$MAX_ATTEMPTS)\"\n              exit 0\n            elif [[ \"$HTTP_CODE\" =~ ^(401|403|404)$ ]]; then\n              echo \"::error::Grafana returned HTTP $HTTP_CODE — check GRAFANA_URL and GRAFANA_API_KEY\"\n              exit 1\n            fi\n            echo \"Grafana not ready (HTTP $HTTP_CODE), waiting ${INTERVAL}s... ($i/$MAX_ATTEMPTS)\"\n            sleep \"$INTERVAL\"\n          done\n          echo \"::error::Grafana Cloud did not become ready after $((MAX_ATTEMPTS * INTERVAL))s\"\n          exit 1"

Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive view. Let me create a final summary report:

## Summary: SDK E2E and Load Test Suites

Based on my search through the repository, here are the **SDK-driven E2E and load test suites**:

### 1. **SDK E2E Tests** (`/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/sdk-e2e/`)

**Type:** Direct SDK testing (no browser/UI)
**Framework:** Vitest
**Runtime:** ~11 seconds
**Test count:** 83 tests across 10 suites

**Test suites (in `/src/suites/`):**
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/sdk-e2e/src/suites/vault-lifecycle.test.ts` — Vault initialization, duplication, GET operations, auth errors
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/sdk-e2e/src/suites/folder-crud.test.ts` — Create, nested folders, rename, move, delete, 20-folder stress test
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/sdk-e2e/src/suites/file-operations.test.ts` — Upload/download round-trips (text, 50KB, 500KB), rename, delete
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/sdk-e2e/src/suites/data-integrity.test.ts` — Byte-for-byte round-trip verification for 8 sizes (100B–500KB), unicode, nested structures
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/sdk-e2e/src/suites/bin-operations.test.ts` — loadBin, deleteToBin, restore, permanentDelete, emptyBin, IPNS persistence
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/sdk-e2e/src/suites/error-cases.test.ts` — Unloaded folder operations, BinNotLoadedError, event emission, post-destroy behavior
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/sdk-e2e/src/suites/share-operations.test.ts` — Share creation, received/sent lists, key unwrap, revocation
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/sdk-e2e/src/suites/invite-link.test.ts` — Create, public status, claim, double-claim prevention, revoke
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/sdk-e2e/src/suites/ipns-consistency.test.ts` — Sequence number monotonicity, IPNS publish/resolve/reload persistence
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/sdk-e2e/src/suites/concurrent-operations.test.ts` — Rapid folder creation, interleaved uploads, create+upload sequences, rapid moves

**Supporting files:**
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/sdk-e2e/src/fixtures/test-harness.ts` — Core account provisioning via `/auth/test-login`, vault initialization, cleanup
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/sdk-e2e/src/fixtures/multi-account.ts` — Multi-account test fixtures
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/sdk-e2e/src/helpers/assertions.ts` — Test assertion utilities
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/sdk-e2e/src/helpers/data-generators.ts` — Test data generation
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/sdk-e2e/vitest.config.ts` — Config: 120s test timeout, 60s hook timeout, no concurrency

**npm scripts:**
```json
"test": "vitest run --no-coverage",
"test:watch": "vitest",
"test:single": "vitest run --no-coverage --testPathPattern"
```

---

### 2. **Load Tests** (`/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/load/`)

**Type:** Performance testing with concurrent client pools
**Framework:** Vitest (Node.js native, not k6)
**Runtime:** Variable (10+ minutes per scenario)
**Scenarios:** 5 different load test scenarios

**Test scenarios (in `/src/scenarios/`):**
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/load/src/scenarios/upload-throughput.test.ts` — 10 clients × 20 file uploads each; measures upload pipeline + IPNS publish throughput
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/load/src/scenarios/ipns-publish-storm.test.ts` — 20 clients × 50 folder create-rename-delete cycles; tests IPNS publish contention
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/load/src/scenarios/mixed-workload.test.ts` — 5 clients × 45 mixed operations (weighted distribution of CRUD); tests realistic usage
- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/load/src/scenarios/sustained-load.test.ts` — 5 clients × 2 ops/sec for 300s; tests latency stability over time

### 3. **Performance Testing Configuration**

**Rate limiting bypass strategy:**

- **Production:** No bypass (secret not configured)

- `SDK_E2E_API_URL` — SDK E2E API endpoint

**SDK E2E automation:**

**Web E2E + Playwright load test:**

- Runs via: `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/web-e2e test` or `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/web-e2e test:headed`

### 5. **Documentation**

- `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/TESTING_STRATEGY.md` — Complete testing strategy guide including rate limiting design, instance-scoped axios pattern, why Node.js instead of k6

**Key design decisions:**
- **No singleton axios:** Each `CipherBoxClient` creates its own instance → eliminates multi-account token contamination
- **Node.js over k6:** k6's Go runtime doesn't support full crypto ecosystem; SDK uses `@noble/*` and Web Crypto API
- **Tiered throttle handling:** Abuse protection (disabled for tests) vs. backpressure signal (preserved in load tests)

## Summary Report: IPNS Performance Metrics, Baselines, and Load Test Configurations

Based on a thorough search of the CipherBox codebase, I've identified all IPNS performance documentation, metrics infrastructure, and load test configurations. Here's what I found:

### 1. **Phase 18 Performance Baselines** (PRIMARY SOURCE)

**Location:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/.planning/baselines/18-performance-baselines.md`

**Key Metrics Captured (2026-03-07/08, Staging):**

#### Client-Side Timings (curl round-trip):
- **IPNS Resolve**: p50=147ms, p95=224ms, p99=278ms
- **IPFS Pin (upload)**: p50=138ms, p95=218ms, p99=227ms
- **IPFS Cat (download)**: p50=133ms, p95=215ms, p99=219ms

#### Server-Side Histograms (Prometheus bucket analysis):

| Operation | Source/Type | Count | p50 | p95 | p99 | Mean | Notes |
|-----------|-------------|-------|------|--------|--------|---------|----------------------|
| **publish** | -- | 1367 | 180ms | 519ms | 904ms | 196ms | Kubo IPNS publish — dominant bottleneck |
| **resolve** | network | 529 | 135ms | 284ms | 488ms | 126ms | Kubo DHT lookup (cache miss) |
| **resolve** | db | 84 | 35ms | 93ms | 187ms | 36ms | DB cache hit |
| **resolve** (fb) | db | 239 | 23ms | 84ms | 230ms | 32ms | DB fallback (Kubo failed) |
| **resolve** | network errors | 42 | 231ms | 650ms | 930ms | 251ms | Timeouts/failures |
| **pin** | -- | 1923 | 8ms | 18ms | 31ms | 8ms | Very fast for small files |
| **cat** | -- | 704 | 2ms | 5ms | 9ms | 2ms | Sub-5ms for cached content |

#### HTTP API Performance by Route:
- `POST /ipfs/upload`: p50=8ms, p95=45ms, p99=50ms (1923 requests)
- `GET /ipfs/:cid`: p50=5ms, p95=10ms, p99=10ms (704 requests)
- `GET /ipns/resolve` [200]: p50=50ms, p95=245ms, p99=467ms (852 requests)
- `GET /ipns/resolve` [404]: p50=231ms, p95=650ms, p99=930ms (42 requests)
- `POST /ipns/publish`: p50=165ms, p95=477ms, p99=871ms (621 requests)
- `POST /ipns/publish-batch`: p50=275ms, p95=793ms, p99=959ms (373 requests)

**Methodology:**
- Single-client baseline: 20 measured iterations + 3 warmup per operation
- 5-client concurrent load test: ~377 ops across 5 concurrent users
- Captured via `scripts/baseline-benchmark.sh` (curl) and Prometheus scraping
- 10KB file size for benchmark uploads/downloads

---

### 2. **Phase 19 IPNS Resolution Improvement Research**

**Location:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/.planning/phases/19-ipns-resolution-improvement/19-RESEARCH.md`

**Key Findings:**
- Recommends replacing external `delegated-ipfs.dev` with self-hosted **Someguy v0.11.1**
- Someguy runs its own embedded libp2p DHT client
- For staging: use `SOMEGUY_DHT=standard` (avoids 2GB+ memory of accelerated mode)
- Proposed new histograms:
  - `cipherbox_ipns_resolve_duration_seconds` — labels: `source` (network/db_cache/network_stale)
  - `cipherbox_ipns_publish_duration_seconds` — labels: `outcome` (success/error/timeout)

### 6. **Metrics Collection & Reporting**

**Location:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/load/src/harness/`

**MetricsCollector** (`metrics.ts`):
- Records individual operation samples: `{operation, durationMs, success, bytes, timestamp}`
- Computes aggregates: count, errors, p50/p95/p99/min/max/avg, throughput ops/sec
- Supports optional bytes tracking for throughput calculation

**Reporter** (`reporter.ts`):
- `printSummary()` — human-readable console table with operation latencies
- `toJsonReport()` — machine-readable JSON export for CI pipelines
- Formats: milliseconds (<1s) or seconds (≥1s)

**Sample Output:**
```
Operation          Count Errors p50        p95        p99        Max
createFolder       225   0     15ms       45ms       89ms       150ms
uploadFile         450   1     250ms      680ms      920ms      1200ms
deleteItem         180   2     8ms        20ms       35ms        60ms
```

---

### 7. **Rate Limiting & Testing Strategy**

**Location:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/TESTING_STRATEGY.md`

**Throttle Handling:**
- **Local/CI**: `NODE_ENV=test` relaxes limits to 200 req/s, 10k req/min
- **Staging**: `X-Throttle-Bypass` header bypasses global rate limit for performance tests
- **Production**: No bypass available

**Horizontal Scaling Notes:**
- Current staging: single-node per-IP throttle
- Load tests measure per-node behavior (relevant for single staging)
- Production will distribute across multiple source IPs

**Why Node.js (not k6):**
- k6 doesn't support full Node.js crypto (Web Crypto, `@noble/*`, `eciesjs`)
- CipherBoxClient is pure TypeScript/ESM
- Network I/O is bottleneck, not CPU

---

### 8. **Phase 18 Research Documentation**

**Location:** `/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/.planning/phases/18-performance-instrumentation/18-RESEARCH.md`

**Bucket Strategy (Two-Tier):**
- **Fast tier** (DB operations): 1ms → 500ms
- **Slow tier** (Network): 50ms → 30s
- **Extended tier** (TEE batches): 1s → 120s
- Exponential spacing throughout: ~2-2.5x between buckets

**Critical Pitfalls Documented:**
1. High cardinality labels (userId, ipnsName, cid) cause Prometheus OOM
2. Bucket boundaries not covering expected range makes histograms useless
3. Alloy network isolation — Kubo needs 0.0.0.0 binding, not 127.0.0.1
4. Kubo /debug/metrics requires API port 5001, not gateway 8080
5. Timing wrong layer (controller vs service) misses actual operation duration
6. Dashboard panel Y-axis collisions when adding new panels

---

## Key Insights

1. **IPNS Publish is the Bottleneck**: ~519ms p95 (904ms p99) is entirely in Kubo — negligible API overhead
2. **DB Cache Fallback is Effective**: Resolves in 23-35ms p50, handles 74% of resolve calls
3. **Pin/Cat Operations are Fast**: 2-8ms p50 server-side; client-side timing ~130ms is network latency
4. **5-Client Concurrent Load**: Drove ~1,300 IPNS publishes through Kubo, p95 reached 793ms for batch publishes
5. **Under Contention**: Publish-batch p95 increased from 477ms (single) to 793ms (5-client), showing publish queue impact
6. **Load Test Infrastructure is Mature**: 5 parameterized scenarios, JSON export, per-operation metrics, no shared singleton state

```
     1→/**
     2→ * Load Test Client Pool
     3→ *
     4→ * Manages N CipherBoxClient instances, each with its own test account.
     5→ * Creates accounts in parallel, distributes workloads, and collects metrics.
     6→ *
     7→ * Reuses the shared createTestAccount/deleteTestAccount from sdk-e2e
     8→ * to avoid duplicating the 5-step account provisioning sequence.
     9→ */
    10→
    11→import {
    12→  createTestAccount,
    13→  deleteTestAccount,
    14→  type TestAccount,
    15→} from '../../../sdk-e2e/src/fixtures/test-harness';
    16→import { MetricsCollector, type OperationMetrics } from './metrics';
    17→import { printSummary, toJsonReport } from './reporter';
    18→
    19→const API_URL = process.env.LOAD_TEST_API_URL ?? 'http://localhost:3000';
    20→const SECRET = process.env.LOAD_TEST_SECRET ?? 'e2e-test-secret-do-not-use-in-production';
    21→
    22→export interface PoolClient extends TestAccount {
    23→  id: number;
    24→  metrics: MetricsCollector;
    25→}
    26→
    27→export interface ClientPoolOptions {
    28→  clientCount: number;
    29→  label: string;
    30→}
    31→
    32→/**
    33→ * Create a pool of N authenticated CipherBoxClient instances.
    34→ *
    35→ * Each client has its own test account, vault, metrics collector, and
    36→ * instance-scoped axios (no shared singleton).
    37→ */
    38→export async function createClientPool(opts: ClientPoolOptions): Promise<PoolClient[]> {
    39→  const { clientCount, label } = opts;
    40→  console.log(`Creating ${clientCount} test accounts for "${label}"...`);
    41→  const start = performance.now();
    42→
    43→  // Create accounts in parallel batches of 5 to avoid overwhelming the API
    44→  const clients: PoolClient[] = [];
    45→  const batchSize = 5;
    46→
    47→  for (let batch = 0; batch < clientCount; batch += batchSize) {
    48→    const batchEnd = Math.min(batch + batchSize, clientCount);
    49→    const promises = [];
    50→
    51→    for (let i = batch; i < batchEnd; i++) {
    52→      promises.push(
    53→        createTestAccount({
    54→          apiUrl: API_URL,
    55→          secret: SECRET,
    56→          label: `${label}-${i}`,
    57→          emailPrefix: 'load',
    58→        }).then((account): PoolClient => ({ ...account, id: i, metrics: new MetricsCollector() }))
    59→      );
    60→    }
    61→
    62→    const results = await Promise.allSettled(promises);
    63→    for (const result of results) {
    64→      if (result.status === 'fulfilled') {
    65→        clients.push(result.value);
    66→      } else {
    67→        console.warn(`Failed to create pool client: ${result.reason}`);
    68→      }
    69→    }
    70→  }
    71→
    72→  const elapsed = performance.now() - start;
    73→  console.log(
    74→    `Created ${clients.length}/${clientCount} clients in ${(elapsed / 1000).toFixed(1)}s`
    75→  );
    76→
    77→  return clients;
    78→}
    79→
    80→/**
    81→ * Destroy all clients in the pool and delete test accounts.
    82→ */
    83→export async function destroyClientPool(pool: PoolClient[]): Promise<void> {
    84→  console.log(`Cleaning up ${pool.length} test accounts...`);
    85→  for (const pc of pool) {
    86→    pc.client.destroy();
    87→    // Zero harness-owned key material (client.destroy() only clears its internal copies)
    88→    pc.privateKey.fill(0);
    89→    pc.rootFolderKey.fill(0);
    90→    pc.rootIpnsKeypair.privateKey.fill(0);
    91→    await deleteTestAccount(pc, API_URL);
    92→  }
    93→}
    94→
    95→/**
    96→ * Aggregate metrics from all pool clients, print summary, and return metrics.
    97→ * Extracts the repeated boilerplate from every load test scenario.
    98→ */
    99→export async function aggregateAndReport(
   100→  scenarioName: string,
   101→  pool: PoolClient[]
   102→): Promise<OperationMetrics[]> {
   103→  const allMetrics = new MetricsCollector();
   104→
   105→  let minTimestamp = Infinity;
   106→  let maxTimestamp = 0;
   107→
   108→  for (const pc of pool) {
   109→    for (const sample of pc.metrics.getReadonlySamples()) {
   110→      allMetrics.record(sample);
   111→      if (sample.timestamp < minTimestamp) minTimestamp = sample.timestamp;
   112→      if (sample.timestamp > maxTimestamp) maxTimestamp = sample.timestamp;
   113→    }
   114→  }
   115→
   116→  // Set collector timing from sample timestamps for accurate throughput calculation
   117→  allMetrics.setElapsedMs(maxTimestamp > minTimestamp ? maxTimestamp - minTimestamp : 1);
   118→
   119→  const totalDuration = maxTimestamp > minTimestamp ? maxTimestamp - minTimestamp : 0;
   120→  const metrics = allMetrics.getMetrics();
   121→
   122→  printSummary(scenarioName, metrics, totalDuration, pool.length);
   123→
   124→  // Write JSON report to file for CI artifact upload
   125→  const reportJson = toJsonReport(scenarioName, metrics, totalDuration, pool.length);
   126→  console.log('\nJSON Report:\n' + reportJson);
   127→
   128→  try {
   129→    const fs = await import('node:fs/promises');
   130→    const slug = scenarioName.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, '-');
   131→    await fs.writeFile(`metrics-${slug}.json`, reportJson);
   132→  } catch (err) {
   133→    console.warn(`Failed to write metrics report: ${(err as Error).message}`);
   134→  }
   135→
   136→  return metrics;
   137→}
   138→
```

     1→/**
     2→ * Load Test Metrics Collection
     3→ *
     4→ * Collects per-operation latency, throughput, and error counts.
     5→ * Uses performance.now() for high-resolution timing.
     6→ */
     7→
     8→export interface OperationMetrics {
     9→  operation: string;
    10→  count: number;
    11→  errors: number;
    12→  latency: { p50: number; p95: number; p99: number; max: number; min: number; avg: number };
    13→  throughputOpsPerSec: number;
    14→  bytesTransferred?: number;
    15→}
    16→
    17→export interface OperationSample {
    18→  operation: string;
    19→  durationMs: number;
    20→  success: boolean;
    21→  bytes?: number;
    22→  timestamp: number;
    23→}
    24→
    25→export class MetricsCollector {
    26→  private samples: OperationSample[] = [];
    27→  private startTime = 0;
    28→  private endTime = 0;
    29→
    30→  start(): void {
    31→    this.startTime = performance.now();
    32→  }
    33→
    34→  stop(): void {
    35→    this.endTime = performance.now();
    36→  }
    37→
    38→  /** Override elapsed time for aggregated collectors (set from sample timestamps). */
    39→  setElapsedMs(ms: number): void {
    40→    this.startTime = 0;
    41→    this.endTime = ms;
    42→  }
    43→
    44→  record(sample: OperationSample): void {
    45→    this.samples.push(sample);
    46→  }
    47→
    48→  /**
    49→   * Wrap an async operation to automatically record metrics.
    50→   */
    51→  async measure<T>(operation: string, fn: () => Promise<T>, bytes?: number): Promise<T> {
    52→    const start = performance.now();
    53→    try {
    54→      const result = await fn();
    55→      this.record({
    56→        operation,
    57→        durationMs: performance.now() - start,
    58→        success: true,
    59→        bytes,
    60→        timestamp: Date.now(),
    61→      });
    62→      return result;
    63→    } catch (err) {
    64→      this.record({
    65→        operation,
    66→        durationMs: performance.now() - start,
    67→        success: false,
    68→        bytes,
    69→        timestamp: Date.now(),
    70→      });
    71→      throw err;
    72→    }
    73→  }
    74→
    75→  /**
    76→   * Get aggregated metrics per operation type.
    77→   */
    78→  getMetrics(): OperationMetrics[] {
    79→    const elapsed = (this.endTime || performance.now()) - this.startTime;
    80→    const elapsedSec = elapsed / 1000;
    81→
    82→    const grouped = new Map<string, OperationSample[]>();
    83→    for (const s of this.samples) {
    84→      const list = grouped.get(s.operation) ?? [];
    85→      list.push(s);
    86→      grouped.set(s.operation, list);
    87→    }
    88→
    89→    const metrics: OperationMetrics[] = [];
    90→    for (const [op, samples] of grouped) {
    91→      const durations = samples.map((s) => s.durationMs).sort((a, b) => a - b);
    92→      const errors = samples.filter((s) => !s.success).length;
    93→      const totalBytes = samples.reduce((sum, s) => sum + (s.bytes ?? 0), 0);
    94→
    95→      metrics.push({
    96→        operation: op,
    97→        count: samples.length,
    98→        errors,
    99→        latency: {
   100→          min: durations[0] ?? 0,
   101→          avg: durations.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / durations.length,
   102→          p50: percentile(durations, 0.5),
   103→          p95: percentile(durations, 0.95),
   104→          p99: percentile(durations, 0.99),
   105→          max: durations[durations.length - 1] ?? 0,
   106→        },
   107→        throughputOpsPerSec: elapsedSec > 0 ? samples.length / elapsedSec : 0,
   108→        ...(totalBytes > 0 ? { bytesTransferred: totalBytes } : {}),
   109→      });
   110→    }
   111→
   112→    return metrics.sort((a, b) => a.operation.localeCompare(b.operation));
   113→  }
   114→
   115→  /** Total samples collected */
   116→  get totalSamples(): number {
   117→    return this.samples.length;
   118→  }
   119→
   120→  /** Total errors across all operations */
   121→  get totalErrors(): number {
   122→    return this.samples.filter((s) => !s.success).length;
   123→  }
   124→
   125→  /** Raw samples (defensive copy, for JSON export) */
   126→  getRawSamples(): OperationSample[] {
   127→    return [...this.samples];
   128→  }
   129→
   130→  /** Read-only access to samples (no copy, for aggregation loops) */
   131→  getReadonlySamples(): readonly OperationSample[] {
   132→    return this.samples;
   133→  }
   134→}
   135→
   136→function percentile(sorted: number[], p: number): number {
   137→  if (sorted.length === 0) return 0;
   138→  const idx = Math.ceil(p * sorted.length) - 1;
   139→  return sorted[Math.max(0, idx)];
   140→}
   141→
```

     1→/**
     2→ * Mixed Workload
     3→ *
     4→ * Simulates realistic usage: a mix of folder CRUD, file uploads,
     5→ * renames, moves, and deletes with weighted probabilities.
     6→ */
     7→
     8→import type { PoolClient } from '../harness/client-pool';
     9→
    10→export interface MixedWorkloadOptions {
    11→  /** Total number of operations */
    12→  totalOps: number;
    13→  /** Operation weights (sum doesn't need to be 1, they're normalized) */
    14→  weights?: {
    15→    createFolder?: number;
    16→    uploadFile?: number;
    17→    renameItem?: number;
    18→    moveItem?: number;
    19→    deleteItem?: number;
    20→  };
    21→}
    22→
    23→const DEFAULT_WEIGHTS = {
    24→  createFolder: 2,
    25→  uploadFile: 4,
    26→  renameItem: 1,
    27→  moveItem: 1,
    28→  deleteItem: 1,
    29→};
    30→
    31→/**
    32→ * Run a mixed workload on a single client.
    33→ */
    34→export async function runMixedWorkload(pc: PoolClient, opts: MixedWorkloadOptions): Promise<void> {
    35→  const { client, rootIpnsName, metrics } = pc;
    36→  const weights = { ...DEFAULT_WEIGHTS, ...opts.weights };
    37→  const values = Object.values(weights);
    38→  if (values.some((w) => typeof w !== 'number' || w < 0 || !Number.isFinite(w))) {
    39→    throw new Error(`Invalid weights: all values must be non-negative finite numbers`);
    40→  }
    41→  const totalWeight = values.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0);
    42→  if (totalWeight <= 0) {
    43→    throw new Error(`Invalid weights: total weight must be > 0`);
    44→  }
    45→
    46→  // Track created items for rename/move/delete targets
    47→  const folderIds: Array<{
    48→    id: string;
    49→    ipnsName: string;
    50→    folderKey: Uint8Array;
    51→    ipnsPrivateKey: Uint8Array;
    52→  }> = [];
    53→  const fileIds: Array<{ id: string; name: string }> = [];
    54→
    55→  for (let i = 0; i < opts.totalOps; i++) {
    56→    const roll = Math.random() * totalWeight;
    57→    let cumulative = 0;
    58→
    59→    // Pick operation based on weighted random
    60→    if ((cumulative += weights.createFolder) > roll) {
    61→      // Create folder
    62→      try {
    63→        const name = `mix-folder-${pc.id}-${i}`;
    64→        const folder = await metrics.measure('createFolder', () =>
    65→          client.createFolder(rootIpnsName, name)
    66→        );
    67→        folderIds.push(folder);
    68→      } catch {
    69→        /* non-fatal */
    70→      }
    71→    } else if ((cumulative += weights.uploadFile) > roll) {
    72→      // Upload file
    73→      try {
    74→        const size = 1024 + Math.floor(Math.random() * 50_000);
    75→        const data = new Uint8Array(size);
    76→        crypto.getRandomValues(data);
    77→        const fileName = `mix-file-${pc.id}-${i}.bin`;
    78→
    79→        await metrics.measure(
    80→          'uploadFile',
    81→          () => client.uploadFile(rootIpnsName, data, fileName, 'application/octet-stream'),
    82→          size
    83→        );
    84→
    85→        const folder = client.getFolderTree().get(rootIpnsName);
    86→        const child = folder?.children.find((c: any) => c.name === fileName);
    87→        if (child) fileIds.push({ id: child.id, name: fileName });
    88→      } catch {
    89→        /* non-fatal */
    90→      }
    91→    } else if ((cumulative += weights.renameItem) > roll) {
    92→      // Rename a random file
    93→      if (fileIds.length > 0) {
    94→        const idx = Math.floor(Math.random() * fileIds.length);
    95→        const target = fileIds[idx];
    96→        const newName = `${target.name}-r${i}`;
    97→        try {
    98→          await metrics.measure('renameItem', () =>
    99→            client.renameItem(rootIpnsName, target.id, newName)
   100→          );
   101→          fileIds[idx] = { ...target, name: newName };
   102→        } catch {
   103→          /* non-fatal */
   104→        }
   105→      }
   106→    } else if ((cumulative += weights.moveItem) > roll) {
   107→      // Move a file into a folder (if both exist)
   108→      if (fileIds.length > 0 && folderIds.length > 0) {
   109→        const fileIdx = Math.floor(Math.random() * fileIds.length);
   110→        const folderIdx = Math.floor(Math.random() * folderIds.length);
   111→        const targetFolder = folderIds[folderIdx];
   112→
   113→        // Ensure target folder is registered
   114→        if (!client.hasFolder(targetFolder.ipnsName)) {
   115→          client.registerFolder(
   116→            targetFolder.ipnsName,
   117→            targetFolder.folderKey,
   118→            { publicKey: new Uint8Array(0), privateKey: targetFolder.ipnsPrivateKey },
   119→            [],
   120→            1n
   121→          );
   122→        }
   123→
   124→        try {
   125→          await metrics.measure('moveItem', () =>
   126→            client.moveItem(rootIpnsName, targetFolder.ipnsName, fileIds[fileIdx].id)
   127→          );
   128→          fileIds.splice(fileIdx, 1); // Remove from root tracking
   129→        } catch {
   130→          /* non-fatal */
   131→        }
   132→      }
   133→    } else {
   134→      // Delete a random item
   135→      if (fileIds.length > 0) {
   136→        const idx = Math.floor(Math.random() * fileIds.length);
   137→        try {
   138→          await metrics.measure('deleteItem', () =>
   139→            client.deleteItem(rootIpnsName, fileIds[idx].id)
   140→          );
   141→          fileIds.splice(idx, 1);
   142→        } catch {
   143→          /* non-fatal */
   144→        }
   145→      } else if (folderIds.length > 0) {
   146→        const idx = Math.floor(Math.random() * folderIds.length);
   147→        try {
   148→          await metrics.measure('deleteItem', () =>
   149→            client.deleteItem(rootIpnsName, folderIds[idx].id)
   150→          );
   151→          folderIds.splice(idx, 1);
   152→        } catch {
   153→          /* non-fatal */
   154→        }
   155→      }
   156→    }
   157→  }
   158→}

> AGENT

The errors are caught at line 68/89/etc (`catch { /* non-fatal */ }`) but `metrics.measure` records `success: false` when the operation throws. The high error rates suggest the SDK operations themselves are failing. This could be rate limiting, IPNS publish failures, or the sustained-load test (which ran first and took ~6 min) warming up Cloudflare's defenses.

Let me run just the mixed-workload and ipns-publish-storm in isolation — one at a time — to get clean baselines without the sustained-load interference.

> AGENT

The root cause is clear: **Delegated routing publishes to someguy are timing out** — `"This operation was aborted"` means the 10-second `requestTimeoutMs` in the `DelegatedRoutingClient` is being hit on every attempt, all 3 retries fail, and the publish is logged as failed.

However, per the `fix(api): make delegated routing publish fire-and-forget` commit, these should be fire-and-forget — the publish should still succeed from the client's perspective (saved to DB), and the delegated routing failure is just a warning.

Let me check whether the test harness is actually failing due to these warnings or something else.

> TOOL

> AGENT

So the API is functioning correctly — IPNS publishes succeed (saved to DB), and the delegated routing to someguy fails fire-and-forget. The client is getting 200 responses. 

The "errors" in the load test are likely SDK-side issues, not API errors. Let me check what's actually throwing in the SDK operations — probably the client is trying to read back metadata after a publish and failing because IPNS resolution through someguy is also timing out.

Wait — actually, let me reconsider. The delegated routing publish is fire-and-forget since PR #308. But the **resolve** path might not be. Let me check if IPNS resolve is also going through someguy and timing out.

> AGENT

There it is. Someguy's IPNS resolve **times out** — curl exit code 28 is a timeout. And nearly 2000 abort errors in 10 minutes. The someguy sidecar is up and its DHT routing table is populated (169 peers), but **IPNS PUT/GET operations are timing out consistently**.

This is likely why you noticed worse performance than delegated-ipfs.dev — someguy can do provider lookups fine but IPNS operations are broken.

Let me verify with a longer timeout and check if it's truly non-functional or just slow:

> AGENT

30 seconds, zero bytes. Someguy's IPNS endpoint is completely non-functional — it accepts the connection but never returns a response.

So here's the full picture:

1. **Someguy's `/routing/v1/providers/` works fine** (we tested earlier — returned results instantly)
2. **Someguy's `/routing/v1/ipns/` is completely dead** — hangs forever on both GET and PUT
3. **This causes every IPNS publish to fail** after 3 retries x 10s timeout = 30s wasted per publish
4. **The API falls back to DB cache** for resolves, so reads still work
5. **The load test "errors" are SDK-side** — likely from stale folder metadata after failed IPNS round-trips

This confirms your earlier observation — someguy performs worse than delegated-ipfs.dev. The IPNS implementation in someguy v0.11.1 appears to be broken or incompatible with our usage pattern.

**Recommendation:** Switch `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL` back to `https://delegated-ipfs.dev` on staging (or remove it entirely and rely on Kubo's native routing), then re-run the load tests for clean baselines. Want me to make that switch?

Based on the search results, here's the information about IPFS Kubo's latest release versions:

The latest stable release of Kubo is version 0.39.0. Additionally, Kubo v0.40.0 and v0.40.1 releases are also available.

Kubo 0.39 makes self-hosting practical on consumer hardware and home networks, with the DHT sweep provider (now default) announcing content to the network without traffic spikes that overwhelm residential connections.

Key features in the latest versions include:

- Automatic UPnP recovery means your node stays reachable after router restarts without manual intervention
- The Amino DHT Sweep provider system, introduced as experimental in v0.38, is now enabled by default
- This release also finalizes the deprecation of the legacy go-ipfs name

You can find the complete release information and downloads at the official GitHub releases page: https://github.com/ipfs/kubo/releases

Based on the search results, I found information about Kubo v0.40.0 and v0.40.1 release changelogs with IPNS improvements:

## Key IPNS Improvements in v0.40.0:

IPNS over PubSub implementation in Kubo is now more reliable. Duplicate messages are rejected even in large networks where messages may cycle back after the in-memory cache expires.

Kubo now persists the maximum seen sequence number per peer to the datastore, providing stronger duplicate detection that survives node restarts. This addresses message flooding issues reported in #9665.

IPNS over PubSub is opt-in via Ipns.UsePubsub.

The release also introduced new features for IPNS record management: You can now backup, restore, and share IPNS records without needing the private key.

## v0.40.1 Release:

This is a Windows bugfix release. If you run Kubo on Windows, v0.40.0 can crash after running for a while. The daemon starts fine and works normally at first, but eventually hits a memory corruption in Go's network I/O layer and dies. This is likely caused by an upstream Go 1.26 regression in overlapped I/O handling. This patch release downgrades the Go toolchain from 1.26 to 1.25, which does not have this bug.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

> AGENT

Here's what I found:

**Someguy:** We're on **v0.11.1**, and **v0.11.0 is the latest stable release** (Nov 2025). So v0.11.1 is actually a minor patch *ahead* of the latest tagged release — there's no newer version to bump to. A version bump won't help here.

**Kubo:** We're on **v0.34.0**, and the latest is **v0.40.1**. That's a significant gap — 6 minor versions behind. Notable improvements in the newer versions:

- **v0.38+**: Amino DHT Sweep provider (now default in v0.39) — better content announcement without traffic spikes
- **v0.39**: Self-hosting improvements for consumer hardware, automatic UPnP recovery
- **v0.40**: IPNS over PubSub reliability fixes, persists max sequence number to datastore for better duplicate detection, IPNS record backup/restore without private key
- **v0.40.1**: Windows-only bugfix (not relevant for our Linux staging)

Given that someguy's IPNS endpoint is completely non-functional (hangs indefinitely on both PUT and GET), and Kubo v0.40 has specific IPNS improvements, I'd suggest:

1. **Bump Kubo from v0.34.0 to v0.40.0** in the staging compose file
2. **Either remove someguy or switch `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL` back to `https://delegated-ipfs.dev`** since someguy's IPNS support appears broken
3. Re-run load tests for clean baselines

Want me to proceed with both changes on a single branch?

Sources:
- [Someguy Releases](https://github.com/ipfs/someguy/releases)
- [Kubo Releases](https://github.com/ipfs/kubo/releases)
- [Kubo v0.40.0 Release](https://github.com/ipfs/kubo/releases/tag/v0.40.0)
- [Kubo v0.39 Changelog](https://github.com/ipfs/kubo/blob/master/docs/changelogs/v0.39.md)
- [Someguy Environment Variables](https://github.com/ipfs/someguy/blob/main/docs/environment-variables.md)

     1→import { Injectable, ExecutionContext } from '@nestjs/common';
     2→import { ThrottlerGuard } from '@nestjs/throttler';
     3→import { timingSafeEqual } from 'node:crypto';
     4→
     5→/**
     6→ * Extends ThrottlerGuard with a bypass header for test environments.
     7→ *
     8→ * When THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET is set, NODE_ENV is not 'production', and
     9→ * a request includes a matching X-Throttle-Bypass header, rate limiting
    10→ * is skipped. This allows SDK E2E and load tests to run against staging
    11→ * without hitting 429s.
    12→ *
    13→ * Security:
    14→ *   - Production always enforces rate limits (NODE_ENV check)
    15→ *   - If the env var is unset/empty, the header is ignored entirely
    16→ *   - Uses crypto.timingSafeEqual to prevent timing attacks
    17→ */
    18→@Injectable()
    19→export class BypassableThrottlerGuard extends ThrottlerGuard {
    20→  async canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): Promise<boolean> {
    21→    const secret = process.env.THROTTLE_BYPASS_SECRET;
    22→
    23→    if (secret && process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production') {
    24→      const request = context.switchToHttp().getRequest();
    25→      const rawHeader = request.headers['x-throttle-bypass'];
    26→      const header = Array.isArray(rawHeader) ? rawHeader[0] : rawHeader;
    27→      if (header && safeEqual(header, secret)) {
    28→        return true;
    29→      }
    30→    }
    31→
    32→    return super.canActivate(context);
    33→  }
    34→}
    35→
    36→/** Constant-time string comparison using Node.js crypto. */
    37→function safeEqual(a: string, b: string): boolean {
    38→  const bufA = Buffer.from(a);
    39→  const bufB = Buffer.from(b);
    40→  if (bufA.length !== bufB.length) return false;
    41→  return timingSafeEqual(bufA, bufB);
    42→}
    43→
```
