The full changelog is available in the GitHub release notes at https://github.com/ipfs/kubo/releases/tag/v0.40.0 and detailed changelogs at https://github.com/ipfs/kubo/blob/master/docs/changelogs/v0.40.md (linked from release planning). Breaking changes and potential API compatibility impacts: 1. IPIP-524: Gateway codec conversion disabled by default. Gateways no longer auto-convert between codecs (e.g., dag-json to raw). Clients must request raw blocks (?format=raw or Accept: application/vnd.ipld.raw) and handle conversion. Previously enabled behavior is now off; enable via config if needed. 2. IPIP-523: ?format= query parameter takes precedence over Accept header in gateways. Deterministic for caching, but breaks cases where Accept header overrode ?format=. 3. UnixFS CID Profiles (IPIP-499): New import profiles (unixfs-v1-2025 default modern, unixfs-v0-2015 for legacy CIDv0). test-cid-v1 and test-cid-v1-wide profiles removed. HAMT sharding threshold fixed (256 KiB directories stay basic, not HAMT). May change CIDs for imports unless profile applied: ipfs config profile apply unixfs-v1-2025. 4. Block size limits raised to 2MiB for ipfs block put/dag import without --allow-big-block. 5. Routing V1 HTTP API exposed by default at http://127.0.0.1:8080/routing/v1 (delegated routing for light clients). Disable via Gateway.ExposeRoutingAPI if unwanted. 6. Badger v1 datastore slated for removal later in 2026; migrate to pebble or levelds. Other notes: Go 1.26 (lower memory/GC), automatic cleanup of interrupted ipfs add/dag import, IPNS PubSub more reliable, new commands (ipfs name get/put, swarm addrs autonat, diag datastore). Overall, minor version bump suggests API compatibility preserved for core RPC/CLI, but gateway and import behaviors changed. Test gateways and imports post-upgrade. v0.40.1 patch fixes Windows crash (Go 1.25 downgrade).\n\nCitations:\n\n- 1: https://github.com/ipfs/kubo/releases/tag/v0.40.0\n- 2: https://newreleases.io/project/github/ipfs/kubo/release/v0.40.0\n- 3: https://chainrelease.info/chains/ipfs~kubo/releases/116\n- 4: https://github.com/ipfs/kubo/issues/11008\n\n---\n\n🌐 Web query:\n\n`Kubo upgrade from v0.34.0 to v0.40.0 migration guide configuration changes`\n\n💡 Result:\n\nTo upgrade Kubo from v0.34.0 to v0.40.0, follow these steps for a smooth migration, focusing on configuration changes. There is no single dedicated migration guide covering all versions, but key changes are handled via automatic repository migrations and config updates documented in release notes and PRs. ## Step 1: Backup Your Repository Always back up your IPFS repository before upgrading: ipfs repo backup /path/to/backup ## Step 2: Download and Install Kubo v0.40.0 Download from https://github.com/ipfs/kubo/releases/tag/v0.40.0. Verify checksums. ## Step 3: Run Repository Migrations Kubo performs automatic migrations on daemon start. Key migration between v0.34.0 (repo v17) and v0.40.0 (repo v18+): - Repo v17 to v18 (introduced in v0.38): Unifies Provider and Reprovider config sections into Provide.DHT. Automatic migration preserves settings: | Old Config | New Config | |------------|------------| | Provider.Enabled | Provide.Enabled | | Provider.WorkerCount | Provide.DHT.MaxWorkers (default: 16) | | Reprovider.Strategy | Provide.Strategy (\"flat\" auto-converts to \"all\") | | Reprovider.Interval | Provide.DHT.Interval | Run manually if needed: ipfs repo migrate ipfs daemon --migrate Other migrations (e.g., v16 to v17 in v0.37) are embedded and automatic. ## Step 4: Review and Update Config Check ~/.ipfs/config post-migration: ipfs config - Verify Provide.* sections. - v0.34 introduced Provider.Enabled (now Provide.Enabled); disable if not providing content: ipfs config Provide.Enabled false. - New in v0.40: UnixFS CID Profiles (opt-in): ipfs config profile apply unixfs-v1-2025. - Badger v1 datastore removal planned; migrate to Pebble if using. ## Step 5: Test and Start Daemon ipfs daemon Monitor logs for issues. Test core functions: add, cat, pin, etc. ## Additional Changes Across Versions - CLI/RPC: New commands (ipfs name get|put, ipfs swarm addrs autonat, ipfs p2p listen --foreground). Deprecated: bitswap reprovide (use routing reprovide). - Gateway: ?format= precedence over Accept header (v0.40); configurable request limits. - Dependencies: Go 1.26 (v0.40), libp2p updates—improved reliability. - Datastore: Pebble updates; Badger v1 deprecated. No breaking changes requiring manual config edits beyond auto-migration. For full changelogs: https://github.com/ipfs/kubo/releases. If issues arise, check repo version (ipfs repo version) and migrate explicitly.\n\nCitations:\n\n- 1: https://github.com/ipfs/kubo/releases\n- 2: https://github.com/ipfs/kubo/releases/tag/v0.40.0\n- 3: https://github.com/ipfs/kubo/pull/10951\n- 4: https://newreleases.io/project/github/ipfs/kubo/release/v0.40.0\n- 5: https://github.com/ipfs/kubo/pull/10804\n- 6: https://github.com/ipfs/kubo/issues/10968\n- 7: https://github.com/ipfs/kubo/blob/master/docs/changelogs/v0.34.md\n- 8: https://newreleases.io/project/github/ipfs/kubo/release/v0.34.0\n\n---\n\n\n\n</details>\n\n**Address breaking changes before deploying to production.**\n\nThe upgrade from v0.34.0 to v0.40.0 introduces multiple breaking changes that require verification:\n\n1. **Gateway codec conversion disabled by default** (IPIP-524): Clients expecting automatic codec conversion (e.g., dag-json to raw) will fail. Test all gateway consumers and configure manual conversion if needed.\n\n2. **Query parameter precedence changed** (IPIP-523): The `?format=` parameter now overrides the Accept header. Verify client requests don't conflict with this behavior.\n\n3. **UnixFS CID profiles changed** (IPIP-499): New default profile may alter import CIDs. If deterministic CIDs are critical, apply the `unixfs-v1-2025` profile explicitly.\n\n4. **Routing V1 API exposed by default**: A new HTTP endpoint is available at `http://127.0.0.1:8080/routing/v1` by default. Confirm this exposure aligns with security requirements.\n\n5. **Config migration** (v17 → v18): Provider/Reprovider config sections auto-migrate to Provide.DHT. Verify the migrated config post-upgrade via `ipfs config`.\n\nTest gateway behavior, client integrations, and data imports before production deployment. All IPFS operations go through the Kubo RPC API (`/api/v0/cat`, `/api/v0/add`), not the gateway.\n\n2. **Query parameter precedence (IPIP-523)**: Same — gateway-only change, not relevant.\n\n3. **UnixFS CID profiles (IPIP-499)**: We store encrypted blobs with `pin=true` via the RPC API. CID determinism isn't a concern since we track CIDs in our DB/IPNS metadata, not recompute them.\n\n4. **Routing V1 API exposed on gateway**: The gateway port (8080) is bound to `127.0.0.1` in staging compose — not externally accessible. And we intentionally run someguy for delegated routing anyway.\n\n5. **Config migration (v17→v18)**: Kubo handles this automatically on daemon start. > DEVELOPER

there is also apparently a patch release v0.40.1 that coderabbit says included stability improvements.

> AGENT

From our earlier research, v0.40.1 is specifically a **Windows-only bugfix** — it downgrades the Go toolchain from 1.26 to 1.25 to fix a memory corruption crash in Go's overlapped I/O on Windows. CodeRabbit's summary ("stability improvements") is misleading.

Our staging and Docker host are both Linux, so v0.40.1 doesn't apply. Sticking with v0.40.0 is the right call — it uses Go 1.26 which has better memory/GC characteristics on Linux.

> AGENT

The key line is:

> `No user facing commits found since e35f6ba — skipping`

Release Please considers `build` commits as non-user-facing by default. It only triggers releases for `feat` (minor bump) and `fix` (patch bump). The `build` type doesn't appear in changelogs or trigger version bumps in the default configuration.

Since this is a significant infrastructure upgrade you want a release for, the commit type should be `feat` instead. Let me check what the release-please config allows.

> AGENT

No custom `changelog-sections` configured, so it uses the defaults — only `feat` and `fix` trigger releases. Two options:

1. **Change the commit to `feat(docker):`** — simplest, accurately reflects that this is a new capability (Kubo v0.40 features)
2. **Add `build` to the release-please changelog sections** — more correct long-term but a config change

Since you want a release now, option 1 is quickest. Want me to amend the commit to `feat(docker): upgrade Kubo from v0.34.0 to v0.40.0`?

5. If it works locally — it was a staging-specific issue (networking, resource constraints, etc.)

Either outcome gives us a clear answer on whether someguy is viable at all or if we should stick with delegated-ipfs.dev long-term. Want me to go ahead?

```json
  "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-03-23T00:38:32.141Z\ntitle: Investigate someguy IPNS routing failure locally\narea: infra\nfiles:\n  - docker/docker-compose.yml\n  - docker/docker-compose.staging.yml\n  - apps/api/src/ipns/delegated-routing.client.ts\n---\n\n## Problem\n\nSomeguy v0.11.1 (latest release) has completely non-functional IPNS endpoints on staging. The `/routing/v1/providers/` endpoint works fine (returns results instantly), but `/routing/v1/ipns/` hangs indefinitely on both PUT and GET — curl times out after 30s with zero bytes received.\n\nThis causes every delegated routing publish to fail after 3 retries x 10s timeout. The API falls back to DB cache for resolves, but publishes waste ~30s per attempt. Nearly 2,000 abort errors were observed in a 10-minute window during load testing.\n\nStaging has been reverted to `https://delegated-ipfs.dev` (PR #322) and someguy removed from the compose stack.\n\n## Solution\n\nReproduce and diagnose locally using the Docker host (192.168.133.114) with Kubo v0.40.0:\n\n1. Add someguy v0.11.1 to `docker/docker-compose.yml`\n2. Point API `DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL` at `http://192.168.133.114:<port>`\n3. Test IPNS endpoint directly: `curl --max-time 30 http://<someguy>/routing/v1/ipns/<key>`\n4. If it hangs locally too — check someguy GitHub issues for known IPNS bugs, file one if needed\n5. If it works locally — investigate staging-specific factors (networking, resource constraints, DNS)\n6. Run SDK E2E (83 tests) and load tests to validate end-to-end\n\nKey questions:\n- Is this a known someguy bug?\n- Does Kubo v0.40.0 (vs v0.34.0 on staging when someguy was deployed) change anything?\n- Is someguy's DHT mode (`standard` vs `accelerated`) a factor?\n- Are there env vars or config we're missing?\n"
```

> DEVELOPER

I was discussing this in a `btw` conversation just now: Looking at the conversation context, I don't have the full CI pipeline workflow file loaded in this
    session — we only looked at deploy-staging.yml and release-please.yml, not the main CI/checks workflow.

    However, based on general principles and what I know about this monorepo:

    Why it's likely sequential now: The build step probably depends on the same compile/transpile output
    that typecheck produces, or it was just written sequentially without optimizing for parallelism.

    What could run concurrently:
    - Typecheck (pnpm typecheck) and Cargo check are purely static analysis — they don't need build
    artifacts from each other
    - Lint (ESLint, Prettier) is also independent
    - Building shared packages (crypto, core, api-client, sdk-core, sdk) is needed before both tests and the
     API/web builds, but not before typecheck or lint

    A reasonable parallel structure would be:

    Stage 1 (parallel):
      - typecheck
      - lint
      - cargo check
      - build shared packages

    Stage 2 (after shared packages built):
      - build API
      - build web
      - unit tests

    Stage 3 (after builds):
      - SDK E2E tests
      - Web E2E tests

    The key constraint is that tests need built artifacts, but typecheck/lint/cargo-check don't. So yes —
    moving the build to run concurrently with those early checks should shave time off the pipeline. You'd
    want to check the actual .github/workflows/ci.yml (or equivalent) to see the current job dependency
    graph and restructure accordingly.

```
   161→  migration-check:
   162→    name: Migration Drift Check
   163→    needs: [changes]
   164→    if: |
   165→      needs.changes.outputs.src == 'true'
   166→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
   167→    services:
   168→      postgres:
   169→        image: postgres:16-alpine
   170→        env:
   171→          POSTGRES_USER: postgres
   172→          POSTGRES_PASSWORD: postgres
   173→          POSTGRES_DB: cipherbox_test
   174→        ports:
   175→          - 5432:5432
   176→        options: >-
   177→          --health-cmd pg_isready
   178→          --health-interval 10s
   179→          --health-timeout 5s
   180→          --health-retries 5
   181→    steps:
   182→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
   183→
   184→      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4
   185→        with:
   186→          version: 10
   187→
   188→      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
   189→        with:
   190→          node-version: '22'
   191→          cache: 'pnpm'
   192→
   193→      - name: Install dependencies
   194→        run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
   195→
   196→      - name: Run existing migrations
   197→        run: pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api migration:run
   198→        env:
   199→          DB_HOST: localhost
   200→          DB_PORT: 5432
   201→          DB_USERNAME: postgres
   202→          DB_PASSWORD: postgres
   203→          DB_DATABASE: cipherbox_test
   204→          NODE_ENV: test
   205→
   206→      - name: Check for entity/migration drift
   207→        working-directory: apps/api
   208→        run: |
   209→          set +e
   210→          OUTPUT="$(pnpm typeorm migration:generate src/migrations/DriftCheck -d src/data-source.ts 2>&1)"
   211→          STATUS=$?
   212→          set -e
   213→
   214→          # Check if migration:generate failed before inspecting output files
   215→          if [ "$STATUS" -ne 0 ]; then
   216→            if echo "$OUTPUT" | grep -q "No changes in database schema were found"; then
   217→              echo "No entity/migration drift detected."
   218→              exit 0
   219→            fi
   220→            echo "::error::migration:generate failed unexpectedly."
   221→            echo "$OUTPUT"
   222→            exit 1
   223→          fi
   224→
   225→          if ls src/migrations/*DriftCheck*.ts 1>/dev/null 2>&1; then
   226→            # Our hand-written migrations use explicit constraint/index names, but TypeORM
   227→            # expects auto-generated hash-based names. Filter out cosmetic renames and only
   228→            # fail on real structural drift (new/dropped tables, added/removed columns, type changes).
   229→            if grep -qE 'CREATE TABLE|DROP TABLE|ADD COLUMN|DROP COLUMN|ALTER.*TYPE|RENAME COLUMN' src/migrations/*DriftCheck*.ts; then
   230→              echo "::error::Entity/migration drift detected! Entities define schema changes not covered by migrations."
   231→              echo ""
   232→              echo "Generated migration contents:"
   233→              cat src/migrations/*DriftCheck*.ts
   234→              echo ""
   235→              echo "Fix: Run 'pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api typeorm migration:generate src/migrations/<Name> -d src/data-source.ts' and commit the result."
   236→              exit 1
   237→            else
   238→              echo "Only cosmetic constraint/index naming differences detected (not structural drift). OK."
   239→            fi
   240→          else
   241→            echo "No entity/migration drift detected."
   242→          fi
   243→        env:
   244→          DB_HOST: localhost
   245→          DB_PORT: 5432
   246→          DB_USERNAME: postgres
   247→          DB_PASSWORD: postgres
   248→          DB_DATABASE: cipherbox_test
   249→          NODE_ENV: test

   501→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest

   503→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
   504→
   505→      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4

   507→          version: 10
   508→

   527→  cargo-windows:
   528→    name: Cargo Check & Test (Windows)
   529→    needs: [changes, lint]
   530→    if: |
   531→      !failure() && !cancelled() &&
   532→      needs.changes.outputs.desktop == 'true'
   533→    runs-on: windows-latest
   534→    steps:
   535→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
   536→
   537→      - name: Install WinFsp
   538→        shell: powershell
   539→        run: |
   540→          $url = "https://github.com/winfsp/winfsp/releases/download/v2.1/winfsp-2.1.25156.msi"
   541→          $out = "winfsp.msi"
   542→          $expectedHash = "073A70E00F77423E34BED98B86E600DEF93393BA5822204FAC57A29324DB9F7A"
   543→          Invoke-WebRequest -Uri $url -OutFile $out
   544→          $actualHash = (Get-FileHash $out -Algorithm SHA256).Hash
   545→          if ($actualHash -ne $expectedHash) { throw "WinFsp MSI hash mismatch: expected $expectedHash, got $actualHash" }
   546→          Start-Process msiexec.exe -ArgumentList "/i","$out","/qn","INSTALLLEVEL=1000" -Wait -NoNewWindow
   547→
   548→      - name: Create WinFsp MSI placeholder for resource glob
   549→        shell: powershell
   550→        run: New-Item -ItemType File -Force -Path "apps/desktop/src-tauri/resources/winfsp-placeholder.msi"
   551→
   552→      - name: Install Rust toolchain
   553→        run: rustup default stable
   554→
   555→      - uses: actions/cache@v4
   556→        with:
   557→          path: |
   558→            ~/.cargo/registry
   559→            ~/.cargo/git
   560→            apps/desktop/src-tauri/target
   561→          key: windows-cargo-${{ hashFiles('apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.lock') }}
   562→          restore-keys: windows-cargo-
   563→
   564→      - name: Cargo check
   565→        run: cargo check --manifest-path apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml --no-default-features --features winfsp
   566→
   567→      - name: Cargo test
   568→        run: cargo test --manifest-path apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml --no-default-features --features winfsp
   569→
   570→  cargo-macos:
   571→    name: Cargo Check & Test (macOS)
   572→    needs: [changes, lint]
   573→    if: |
   574→      !failure() && !cancelled() &&
   575→      needs.changes.outputs.desktop == 'true'
   576→    runs-on: macos-latest
   577→    steps:
   578→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
   579→
   580→      - name: Install FUSE-T
   581→        run: |
   582→          brew install --cask fuse-t
   583→          sudo mkdir -p /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig
   584→          FUSE_T_PC="/Library/Application Support/fuse-t/pkgconfig/fuse-t.pc"
   585→          if [ ! -f "$FUSE_T_PC" ]; then
   586→            FUSE_T_PC=$(find /Library /usr/local /opt/homebrew -name "fuse-t.pc" 2>/dev/null | head -1)
   587→          fi
   588→          if [ -z "$FUSE_T_PC" ] || [ ! -f "$FUSE_T_PC" ]; then
   589→            echo "::error::fuse-t.pc not found after FUSE-T installation"
   590→            exit 1
   591→          fi
   592→          sudo cp "$FUSE_T_PC" /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig/fuse.pc
   593→          sudo sed -i '' 's/^Version:.*/Version: 2.9.9/' /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig/fuse.pc
   594→
   595→      - name: Install Rust toolchain
   596→        run: rustup default stable
   597→
   598→      - uses: actions/cache@v4
   599→        with:
   600→          path: |
   601→            ~/.cargo/registry
   602→            ~/.cargo/git
   603→            apps/desktop/src-tauri/target
   604→          key: macos-cargo-${{ hashFiles('apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.lock') }}
   605→          restore-keys: macos-cargo-
   606→
   607→      - name: Cargo check
   608→        working-directory: apps/desktop/src-tauri
   609→        run: cargo check --no-default-features --features fuse
   610→        env:
   611→          PKG_CONFIG_PATH: /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig
   612→
   613→      - name: Cargo test
   614→        run: cargo test --manifest-path apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml --no-default-features --features fuse
   615→        env:
   616→          PKG_CONFIG_PATH: /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig
   617→
   618→  cargo-linux:
   619→    name: Cargo Check, Test & Coverage (Linux)
   620→    needs: [changes, lint]
   621→    if: |
   622→      !failure() && !cancelled() &&
   623→      needs.changes.outputs.desktop == 'true'
   624→    runs-on: ubuntu-22.04
   625→    steps:
   626→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
   627→
   628→      - name: Install system dependencies
   629→        run: |
   630→          sudo apt-get update
   631→          sudo apt-get install -y \
   632→            libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev \
   633→            libayatana-appindicator3-dev \
   634→            librsvg2-dev \
   635→            libssl-dev \
   636→            libxdo-dev \
   637→            libfuse3-dev \
   638→            pkg-config \
   639→            build-essential
   640→
   641→      - name: Install Rust toolchain
   642→        run: rustup default stable
   643→
   644→      - uses: actions/cache@v4
   645→        with:
   646→          path: |
   647→            ~/.cargo/registry
   648→            ~/.cargo/git
   649→            apps/desktop/src-tauri/target
   650→          key: linux-cargo-${{ hashFiles('apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.lock') }}
   651→          restore-keys: linux-cargo-
   652→
   653→      - name: Cargo check
   654→        working-directory: apps/desktop/src-tauri
   655→        run: cargo check --no-default-features --features fuse
   656→
   657→      - name: Install cargo-llvm-cov
   658→        run: |
   659→          rustup component add llvm-tools-preview
   660→          cargo install cargo-llvm-cov --locked
   661→
   662→      - name: Cargo test with coverage
   663→        run: >-
   664→          cargo llvm-cov
   665→          --manifest-path apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml
   666→          --no-default-features --features fuse
   667→          --lcov --output-path desktop-lcov.info
   668→
   669→      - name: Upload desktop coverage to Codecov
   670→        uses: codecov/codecov-action@v5
   671→        with:
   672→          token: ${{ secrets.CODECOV_TOKEN }}
   673→          files: desktop-lcov.info
   674→          flags: desktop
   675→          fail_ci_if_error: false
   676→
```

> AGENT

Here's the current dependency graph:

```
changes ──┬── lint (no deps, runs immediately)
          │
          ├── typecheck (waits for changes)
          ├── api-spec (waits for changes)
          ├── migration-check (waits for changes)
          │
          ├── cargo-{macos,windows,linux} (waits for changes + lint)
          │
          ├── test (waits for lint + typecheck)
          │
          ├── sdk-e2e (waits for lint + typecheck + test)
          │
          └── build (waits for EVERYTHING: lint + typecheck + api-spec + test + sdk-e2e + migration-check)
```

The critical path is: `changes → typecheck → test → sdk-e2e → build` — fully sequential.

The `btw` analysis is right. Here's what could be parallelized:

**Currently sequential but independent:**
- `typecheck`, `api-spec`, and `migration-check` all only need `changes` — they already run in parallel (good)
- `test` waits for `typecheck` — but tests need built packages, not typecheck output. Typecheck is a gate, not a dependency
- `build` waits for everything including `sdk-e2e` — but build is just "can it compile", which doesn't depend on test results

**Quick wins:**
1. **`test` doesn't need `typecheck`** — it builds packages itself. Remove `typecheck` from its `needs` (lint is enough as a quality gate)
2. **`build` should run in parallel with `test`/`sdk-e2e`** — it's a compilation check, not dependent on test outcomes
3. **`sdk-e2e` doesn't need `test`** — it's a separate test suite with its own service containers

Also noticed: the `api-spec` and `sdk-e2e` jobs still reference `ipfs/kubo:v0.34.0` — should be bumped to v0.40.0 to match.

Want me to create a todo for the CI parallelization, or tackle it now?

```
   206→      - name: Check for entity/migration drift
   207→        working-directory: apps/api
   208→        run: |
   209→          set +e
   210→          OUTPUT="$(pnpm typeorm migration:generate src/migrations/DriftCheck -d src/data-source.ts 2>&1)"
   211→          STATUS=$?
   212→          set -e
   213→
   214→          # Check if migration:generate failed before inspecting output files
   215→          if [ "$STATUS" -ne 0 ]; then
   216→            if echo "$OUTPUT" | grep -q "No changes in database schema were found"; then
   217→              echo "No entity/migration drift detected."
   218→              exit 0
   219→            fi
   220→            echo "::error::migration:generate failed unexpectedly."
   221→            echo "$OUTPUT"
   222→            exit 1
   223→          fi
   224→
   225→          if ls src/migrations/*DriftCheck*.ts 1>/dev/null 2>&1; then
   226→            # Our hand-written migrations use explicit constraint/index names, but TypeORM
   227→            # expects auto-generated hash-based names. Filter out cosmetic renames and only
   228→            # fail on real structural drift (new/dropped tables, added/removed columns, type changes).
   229→            if grep -qE 'CREATE TABLE|DROP TABLE|ADD COLUMN|DROP COLUMN|ALTER.*TYPE|RENAME COLUMN' src/migrations/*DriftCheck*.ts; then
   230→              echo "::error::Entity/migration drift detected! Entities define schema changes not covered by migrations."
   231→              echo ""
   232→              echo "Generated migration contents:"
   233→              cat src/migrations/*DriftCheck*.ts
   234→              echo ""
   235→              echo "Fix: Run 'pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api typeorm migration:generate src/migrations/<Name> -d src/data-source.ts' and commit the result."
   236→              exit 1
   237→            else
   238→              echo "Only cosmetic constraint/index naming differences detected (not structural drift). OK."
   239→            fi
   240→          else
   241→            echo "No entity/migration drift detected."
   242→          fi

   494→
   495→  build:
   496→    name: Build
   497→    needs: [changes, lint, typecheck, api-spec, test, sdk-e2e, migration-check]
   498→    if: |
   499→      !failure() && !cancelled() &&
   500→      needs.changes.outputs.src == 'true'
   501→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
   502→    steps:
   503→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
   504→
   505→      - uses: pnpm/action-setup@v4
   506→        with:
   507→          version: 10
   508→
   509→      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
   510→        with:
   511→          node-version: '22'
   512→          cache: 'pnpm'
   513→
   514→      - name: Install dependencies
   515→        run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
   516→
   517→      - name: Build packages (excluding desktop)
   518→        run: |
   519→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/crypto build
   520→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/core build
   521→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api-client build
   522→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk-core build
   523→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk build
   524→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api build
   525→          pnpm --filter @cipherbox/web build
   526→
   527→  cargo-windows:
   528→    name: Cargo Check & Test (Windows)
   529→    needs: [changes, lint]
   530→    if: |
   531→      !failure() && !cancelled() &&
   532→      needs.changes.outputs.desktop == 'true'
   533→    runs-on: windows-latest
   534→    steps:
   535→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
   536→
   537→      - name: Install WinFsp
   538→        shell: powershell
   539→        run: |
   540→          $url = "https://github.com/winfsp/winfsp/releases/download/v2.1/winfsp-2.1.25156.msi"
   541→          $out = "winfsp.msi"
   542→          $expectedHash = "073A70E00F77423E34BED98B86E600DEF93393BA5822204FAC57A29324DB9F7A"
   543→          Invoke-WebRequest -Uri $url -OutFile $out
   544→          $actualHash = (Get-FileHash $out -Algorithm SHA256).Hash
   545→          if ($actualHash -ne $expectedHash) { throw "WinFsp MSI hash mismatch: expected $expectedHash, got $actualHash" }
   546→          Start-Process msiexec.exe -ArgumentList "/i","$out","/qn","INSTALLLEVEL=1000" -Wait -NoNewWindow
   547→
   548→      - name: Create WinFsp MSI placeholder for resource glob
   549→        shell: powershell
   550→        run: New-Item -ItemType File -Force -Path "apps/desktop/src-tauri/resources/winfsp-placeholder.msi"
   551→
   552→      - name: Install Rust toolchain
   553→        run: rustup default stable
   554→
   555→      - uses: actions/cache@v4
   556→        with:
   557→          path: |
   558→            ~/.cargo/registry
   559→            ~/.cargo/git
   560→            apps/desktop/src-tauri/target
   561→          key: windows-cargo-${{ hashFiles('apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.lock') }}
   562→          restore-keys: windows-cargo-
   563→
   564→      - name: Cargo check
   565→        run: cargo check --manifest-path apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml --no-default-features --features winfsp
   566→
   567→      - name: Cargo test
   568→        run: cargo test --manifest-path apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml --no-default-features --features winfsp
   569→
   570→  cargo-macos:
   571→    name: Cargo Check & Test (macOS)
   572→    needs: [changes, lint]
   573→    if: |
   574→      !failure() && !cancelled() &&
   575→      needs.changes.outputs.desktop == 'true'
   576→    runs-on: macos-latest
   577→    steps:
   578→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
   579→
   580→      - name: Install FUSE-T
   581→        run: |
   582→          brew install --cask fuse-t
   583→          sudo mkdir -p /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig
   584→          FUSE_T_PC="/Library/Application Support/fuse-t/pkgconfig/fuse-t.pc"
   585→          if [ ! -f "$FUSE_T_PC" ]; then
   586→            FUSE_T_PC=$(find /Library /usr/local /opt/homebrew -name "fuse-t.pc" 2>/dev/null | head -1)
   587→          fi
   588→          if [ -z "$FUSE_T_PC" ] || [ ! -f "$FUSE_T_PC" ]; then
   589→            echo "::error::fuse-t.pc not found after FUSE-T installation"
   590→            exit 1
   591→          fi
   592→          sudo cp "$FUSE_T_PC" /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig/fuse.pc
   593→          sudo sed -i '' 's/^Version:.*/Version: 2.9.9/' /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig/fuse.pc
   594→
   595→      - name: Install Rust toolchain
   596→        run: rustup default stable
   597→
   598→      - uses: actions/cache@v4
   599→        with:
   600→          path: |
   601→            ~/.cargo/registry
   602→            ~/.cargo/git
   603→            apps/desktop/src-tauri/target
   604→          key: macos-cargo-${{ hashFiles('apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.lock') }}
   605→          restore-keys: macos-cargo-
   606→
   607→      - name: Cargo check
   608→        working-directory: apps/desktop/src-tauri
   609→        run: cargo check --no-default-features --features fuse
   610→        env:
   611→          PKG_CONFIG_PATH: /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig
   612→
   613→      - name: Cargo test
   614→        run: cargo test --manifest-path apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml --no-default-features --features fuse
   615→        env:
   616→          PKG_CONFIG_PATH: /usr/local/lib/pkgconfig
   617→
```

> AGENT

Now I have the full picture. Here are the changes:

1. **Bump Kubo v0.34.0 → v0.40.0** in `api-spec`, `test`, and `sdk-e2e` service containers
2. **Parallelize `test`** — remove `typecheck` from its `needs` (it builds packages itself, doesn't need typecheck output)
3. **Parallelize `sdk-e2e`** — remove `test` from its `needs` (independent test suite with own services)
4. **Parallelize `build`** — only needs `changes` + `lint` (it's a compilation check, not dependent on test results)

New dependency graph:
```
changes ──┬── lint (immediate)
          ├── typecheck (immediate)
          ├── api-spec (immediate)
          ├── migration-check (immediate)
          ├── cargo-{macos,windows,linux} (after lint)
          ├── test (after changes + lint)
          ├── sdk-e2e (after changes + lint)
          └── build (after changes + lint)
```

```json
  "description": "Fetch unresolved review threads"
}
```

> AGENT

4 threads from Copilot. Let me triage:

1. **Line 500** (build `if` redundant): Valid — `!failure() && !cancelled()` was needed when `build` depended on test/sdk-e2e, now it's meaningless with only `changes + lint` in `needs`.
2. **Line 253** (PR description mismatch): The description is slightly inaccurate but it's about the PR body, not code. I'll fix the code to match the intent — `typecheck`/`api-spec`/`migration-check` should also wait for `lint` to fail-fast.
3. **Line 256** (test `if` redundant): Same as #1 — clean up the condition.
4. **Line 360** (sdk-e2e `if` redundant): Same pattern.

The right fix: add `lint` to `typecheck`/`api-spec`/`migration-check` needs (fail-fast on lint before expensive jobs), and simplify the `if` conditions on `test`/`sdk-e2e`/`build`.
